- **W and Y.** In "Answer 2's detached bind, and the bind as one verb", W:5336–5512 and Y:5387–5563, Y's line is W's plus 51, and the text is W's but for two lines of the bound's sum (section 9). So T501's W:5372–5383 is Y:5423–5434, and its W:5442–5456 is Y:5493–5507. W:5149 is Y:5200, unchanged. This contract cites Y, the branch's change, and follows it.

B3's `test-plan.md` and its two `specs/` files are Y's byte for byte (checked by this writer with `diff` and `cmp`).

**The writers**, as this contract names them:
- **the guard**: `guard_run` in a prompt's hook. At the prompt that makes the offer it writes the file through `guard_offer` (pin:10960–10971); at each later prompt its offer-site branch answers from the record (Y:5493).
- **the hook**: `guard_answer`'s choice 2, in the hook whose prompt is the answer `2` (Y:5393). T501's line calls its write of `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` the guard's (tasks:581), and T507 places that write in `guard_answer` (tasks:747).
- **the outer**: `guard-bind <uuid>`, the detached child, "the pid of record" (Y:5396).
- **the body**: `guard-bind --body <uuid>`, which the outer runs under `timeout` (Y:5397–5398), or `LANES_BIND_BODY` in its place under the seam (R48). It writes no key of the record. It hands `why=` and `note=` to the outer in its facts file (section 1; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 6).

## 1. The offer file as the bind's record

- **The record is the offer file itself,** `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/lanes/offers/<session uuid>` (Y:5423). `guard_offer_file` names it `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/lanes/offers/<uuid>` (pin:10271–10273). It is "keyed by the transcript, read by the guard at the offer site …, and the session's own fact" (Y:5423).
- **Not a new state file:** "The detached bind's record is the existing offer file in new modes, not a new state file", since "it is keyed by the transcript and the guard already reads it" (Y:6057, row 31).
- **On the mount shared across containers:** "The directory is a bind mount shared across containers …, so the record names its host and container, and liveness is judged only where both match" (Y:5423).
- **Its form:** `key=value` lines (Y:5423). `note=` may repeat, one line per act still owed (Y:5432).
- **Who creates, rewrites and removes it:**
  - **Created** by the guard at the offer, `mode=name-drift` with the offer's identity, its directory made by `mkdir -p` (`guard_offer`, pin:10962–10971). That is today's code, and the bind keeps it, under `umask 077` (below).
    - **`guard_offer`'s write is creation** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885378657, ruling 4, (a); lane 3 reopened point 26 for its reason and ruled it again, below: lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, point 26). At `ae702c74` it wrote the record in place: `> <record>` under `umask 077`, with no temporary file and no `mv`, its writes unchecked and its failure ignored (ae702c74:21742–21753). Since T502's part 23 it makes the record by an exclusive create (below). Its one caller is now `guard_offer_site`, which `guard_run` calls (6253b0ed:21745, :22007, :22063; at `ae702c74`, `guard_run` itself, :21624), and it only ever writes a fresh `name-drift` offer.
    - **At `ae702c74` it could land on a record in two cases:** where the guard's drop of a stale `allow` or `name-drift` record failed, since the drop is `rm -f` and returns 0 whatever it did (ae702c74:20362–20366; its callers here, ae702c74:21623, and :22329 with :21431–21434); and where a record appeared between the guard's read of the file (ae702c74:21417) and this write. Since parts 23 and 28 it lands on none: a stale `allow` or `name-drift` record that a failed drop left, or that is there where `offers/` cannot take the temporary file, is no longer written over or asked over; the prompt is refused, the record is left byte for byte, and each later prompt meets the same record and the same refusal until the drop works, a drop that works being followed by the offer, made once, as before (6253b0ed:21944–21951, :21996–22003, :22062–22066). At `00e2c92e` the words were false where the drop's `rm` failed: `guard_drop_if` returned 0 whatever its `rm` did, so a stale offer was said to be DROPPED, and the refusal promised the question afresh, while the record stayed (lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, SHOULD-1, #165 6894385553). Since T502's part 56, `bf073158` and `42e946ce`, a failed `rm` is said: `guard_drop_if` returns 2 where its `rm` failed and the record is still there (de96ef86:20465–20474, :20494–20502). The stale offer's note then says that it could NOT be removed, and DROPPED only where the drop worked; where the record had changed or gone before the drop's read, it says that nothing was removed (:23451–23466). The offer site's retry, meeting a record whose drop failed (the dangling link's, the allowance's or the stale offer's), refuses with `guard_drop_refuse`'s words, never "asked afresh", at `de96ef86`: "the pending offer could not be recorded: a record of this transcript's is at `<file>`, and it could NOT be removed: `rm` failed on `<file>`, and removing a record needs the offers directory, `<dir>`, to be writable. So the record is still there, this prompt changed nothing, and the next prompt meets the same record. Make `<dir>` writable, or remove `<file>` yourself. Send your prompt again after that.", then the bypass (:20505–20512, :22203–22213, :22229, :22259–22264, :22301–22307). Case 5's drop, where its `rm` fails, still hears the outcome and adds that the bind record could NOT be removed, so the next prompt hears it again (:23011–23028). Since T502's part 60, `f2e545b8`, the refusal says "So the record is still there and nothing was removed, and the next prompt meets the same record." in place of "…this prompt changed nothing…"; it adds "This window keeps the name `<to>` that this answer gave it." where an answer `2` renamed the window; and it names the cause by `guard_rm_cause`. Where the offers directory cannot be written, the cause is "and removing a record needs the offers directory, `<dir>`, to be writable", and the way out "Make `<dir>` writable, or remove `<file>` yourself."; where it can, the cause is "though the offers directory, `<dir>`, is writable, so the file itself refuses removal (an attribute, or a sticky directory's other owner)", and the way out "Remove `<file>` yourself once that is put right.". The stale offer's note and case 5's note name the cause the same way (lane 5's adversarial read of parts 56 to 59, NITs 3 and 4; f2e545b8:20505–20532, :23063–23066, :23501–23503). Since part 60, `GA_DROP_FAILED` is 0 at file scope, so a value the environment carries neither silences the first failed-drop note nor names a failed `rm` that never happened. **The window left,** stated in its comment: within one prompt, where the stale drop failed and the offer site's retry then meets a record another prompt put there meanwhile, the retry's refusal names the failed `rm` on the path though the record there is that newer one, and the next prompt reads it (NIT-2; :22366–22373). Since T502's part 62, `6d2bc47a`, `guard_drop_refuse` says "So the record is still there, and was not removed; the next prompt meets the same record." in place of "…and nothing was removed, and…", since the stale drop's and case 5's `rm` list the `.bind.*` names too, and `rm` goes on past the record where it alone refuses (lane 3's review of `00e2c92e..475a04a4`, NIT-2, #165 6898801339; eb509d24:20523–20529, :20534).
    - **Ruling 4's reason, false at `ae702c74`:** "In both, it replaces only a stale `allow` or `name-drift` offer, never a `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed` record, and a partial write heals itself" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885378657, ruling 4). The guard answers a `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed` record by section 5's cases and returns before `guard_offer` (ae702c74:21421–21425), but that proves only what its one read found (ae702c74:21417). `guard_offer` then truncates and writes with no second read, no lock and no test of what is there (ae702c74:21742–21753). So another invocation can install `binding` in between (ae702c74:22453–22460), and its outer `bound` or `bind-failed` (ae702c74:22754–22828), and this offer then overwrites that newer bind record (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6885771975, S1). A record without `mode` or `from` is still dropped at the next prompt, which asks the question afresh (ae702c74:22311–22315).
    - **Point 26, ruled again:** in lane 3's words, "the creation must not write over a bind record either" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, point 26). T502's part 23 builds it with a create that fails where a file is there, which parts 28, 29, 33, 37, 38 and 40 complete (`af3f547e`; 6253b0ed:21874–21978), so no record is written over by it, and for that no accepted limit remains: lane 3's ruling says such a mechanism "is better, and the limit goes away".
      - **The create:** `guard_offer` writes the offer whole to a temporary file in `offers/`, under `umask 077`, every `printf` checked (00e2c92e:22009–22023). A directory, or a link to one, at the record's path when its `-d` test runs is a record that is there, and `ln` is not run on it (:22024–22028). Otherwise it makes the record by `ln`, one call that fails where the name exists, and takes the link as its own create only where the record is then the very file it wrote (`-ef`), whether `ln` reported 0 or a failure, as an NFS retransmit can (:22029–22039). A directory, or a link to one, made at the path after the `-d` test takes `ln`'s link inside it, as `<dir>/<the temporary file's name>`, and `ln` reports 0. Since T502's part 42, wherever the path is not then the very file written, `guard_offer_unlink_inside` removes that child, and only where it is the very file written (`-ef`), touching nothing else inside the directory; the path is a record that is there, which the site refuses as a directory (:21968–21980, :22034, :22039; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S1). Where hard links are refused, it falls back to bash's `noclobber`: `3>` under `set -C` and `umask 077` is the `O_EXCL` create, and the content is written through that descriptor and never by opening the name again (:22040–22052). Since part 46, "made" is proved on that descriptor while it is still open: the record's path must name the very file it opened (`-ef /dev/fd/3`). Since part 49 it must also be a regular file (`-f`): `noclobber` opens a file that is there and is not regular without `O_EXCL`, so a symbolic link to `/dev/null` put at the path after the test, made where `ln` failed, that nothing is there (:22035) is opened and is the very file the descriptor names, yet it is no create, and gives a 5; and where `/dev/fd/3` cannot be `stat()`ed, as on Linux without `/proc`, the test fails, and that too is a 5, "not made" (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, NIT-4; :22041, :22053–22086). A 5 is a file this writer did not make, or made and that the path no longer names: nothing is removed for it, and a record then there is answered as one that is there. A failed `cat` is a 4, this writer's own partial file, only while the path still names it and it is a regular file, and only a 4 removes the name (:22041, :22062–22092; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, NIT 1). Wherever the create was not made and a record is there, a failed `mktemp` or temporary write included, it writes nothing, says nothing and returns 3 (:22099–22106). Its temporary file is removed, as best effort (:22097), and where it did not make the record, the question it asks says that the offer could not be written (:22131).
      - **A record that is there is answered as the guard's first read would answer it** (`guard_offer_site`, 00e2c92e:22159–22246). A dangling link is dropped, as case 11 drops a record it cannot read, and the retry makes the offer (:22171–22175). Any other path that is not a regular file is the person's to remove, named as such since part 46 (lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, NIT 2): "the pending offer could not be recorded: a record of this transcript's is at `<file>` and could not be replaced, so nothing was written to it.", then, for a directory or a link to one, "It is not a regular file. It is a directory, or a link to one: remove it, then send your prompt again.", and for anything else, "It is not a regular file (a FIFO, a socket or a device, or a link to one): remove it, then send your prompt again." (:22176–22187). A record gone since the failed create is neither answered nor dropped, and the retry makes the offer (:22188–22193). Otherwise it reads the mode once. `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed`: `guard_bind_site`, by section 5's cases (:22196–22198). `allow`: passed with its warning where it still fits; else dropped by `guard_allow_drop`, while it still says `allow` (below). Where that drop finds the record has become another one, it reads it once more: a `name-drift` offer, or a file with no `mode`, is answered by `guard_answer` with this prompt, and an allowance there that fits after that answer's 3 passes, since part 46 (lane 5's held finding H); anything else is answered once by `guard_site_moved` (section 5; :22199–22227). `name-drift`, or no mode: `guard_answer`, with this prompt as its answer, and where that answers 3 with an allowance there that fits, the allowance passes (:22228–22238). Only then is the offer made once more, and that is the whole loop: where the second create fails too, nothing is written, and the prompt is refused with "the pending offer could not be recorded: a record of this transcript's is at `<file>` and could not be replaced, so nothing was written to it. Send your prompt again and the question will be asked afresh." (:22241–22245).
      - **The `ln` call:** the test that no file is there and the create are the one `ln` call, so no record is written over and no reader sees half of one, and the `-ef` test settles the two cases where `ln`'s status and the link disagree (00e2c92e:22029–22039). It leaves the one window from `ln` to a child link's removal, below.
      - **The windows that remain, accepted as limits for v1.3** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, point 26, which asks every remaining window stated with its consequence; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S1 to S3, S6 and NIT 1). Each is given with its exact span and its consequence, as the code at `00e2c92e` shows it, and lane 3 has accepted each as a limit for v1.3, stated with its consequence, in point 26's form (lane 3's rulings, #165 6894116246, point 27's windows; section 10's point 27). Lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, adds windows and consequences that lane 3's ruling does not name, each marked so below, which lane 3 confirms under it (#165 6894714082). Each window is milliseconds wide, not microseconds: the fork and exec of the `mv` or `rm` that ends it takes 5.3 ms, and the `noclobber` windows' subshell adds a fork; one `sed | head` read takes 3.9 ms, and `guard_answer` 22 ms from its entry to its case-11 return, the medians of 30 runs on lane 1's WSL2 host; no `sleep` or signal widens them (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, the windows' width). Those of `guard_offer` are here. The rewrites' and choice 1's are under the rule below, and the drops' and case 5's under the removals; and each message after an I/O failure that says what a record "still" holds is true at the instant of its re-read only (section 5).
        - **From `ln` to a child link's removal** (S1): a directory, or a link to one, made at the record's path after the `-d` test, and then moved away, or removed and made again, before `guard_offer_unlink_inside` removes the child, keeps that child: a second name of this offer's temporary file, 0600, holding this offer's lines alone. No record is written over, and the site then answers whatever is at the path, as above: a directory refused, or, where the path is empty, the offer made by the retry (00e2c92e:21968–21980, :22000–22008, :22034, :22039, :22105–22106). T502's part 57, `796d00c6`, keeps `ln` and states why `link`, which calls `link(2)` with exactly two names and would close this window, is not taken: macOS's `/bin/link` is not verified, and the proof's rounds 7 to 12 depend on the `ln` line; the window stays, as stated, until both are settled (lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, NIT 4; de96ef86:22043–22053).
        - **The `noclobber` fallback's empty instant,** only where hard links are refused: the record exists, empty, from the exclusive open until its content is written (00e2c92e:22040–22052). A guard that reads it then finds no readable `mode` and drops it as an offer it cannot read (section 5's case 11). The write then goes to the unlinked file, so nothing is made again at another mode and no other record is written over, and since part 46 the descriptor's test finds that the path no longer names this writer's file, a 5: the question is asked with its note that the offer could not be written, or, where another invocation's record is there by then, that record is answered as one that is there (:22053–22086, :22105–22106, :22131). Where the reader's `rm` comes only after that test, the test passes, and the writer says the offer was made, with no note, while the record is gone: the next prompt finds no record, and the question is asked afresh (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, NIT-1, which lane 3's ruling on point 27 does not name, confirmed under it by lane 3, #165 6894714082, as lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21 gives it ('Not asked'), a reading lane 3 confirms in its review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e` (spec#65 6898800291, call (a))).
        - **The `noclobber` fallback's status 4, from its test to its `rm`** (NIT 1; lane 5's final check, C), only where hard links are refused: the test that the path still names this writer's partial file is made while the descriptor is open, and the `rm` after it is outside the descriptor, so a record put in place between them is removed, while its `.bind.*` files are not (00e2c92e:22087–22092). The next prompt finds no record and asks the question afresh. Where the record so removed is a `binding`, its outer runs nothing, its start rewrite finding no record (part 21), or, where its body had started, the bind runs on, and how it ends goes only to its `.out`, which is kept, since `--own` finds no record (section 7); or, where the `rm` lands inside that outer's own start or outcome rewrite, its `mv` makes the record again (section 1) (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, NIT-1, which lane 3's ruling on point 27 does not name, confirmed under it by lane 3, #165 6894714082, as lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21 gives it ('Not asked'), a reading lane 3 confirms in its review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e` (spec#65 6898800291, call (a))).
        - **The `noclobber` fallback, from its test that nothing is there to its open** (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, NIT-4), only where hard links are refused: bash's `noclobber` opens a file that is there and is not a regular file without `O_EXCL` (00e2c92e:22035, :22040–22041). A FIFO put at the path in that instant blocks the open, for a reader, until the hook's time budget ends it, as it did before part 46: in the code's words, a window left, and not a new one. It has two further consequences, which T502's part 51 states: the temporary file `.<uuid>.XXXXXX` is left in `offers/`, since the `rm` after the create never runs, and the prompt goes however Claude Code treats a hook that its timeout ends, which this contract does not know (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 48 to 50, NIT-C; :22068–22073, :22097). What can outlive that timeout is stated as lane 2's probe found it, since T502's part 54: where the signal goes to the hook's top process alone, the `( guard_run )` subshell and the blocked writer survive, re-parented, holding the hook's stdout and stderr pipes. They hold no lock, write no record and rename nothing; the next prompts never open the FIFO, each read testing `[ -f ]` first, and the offer site refuses it. They stay until they are killed or the machine restarts, or until something opens the FIFO for reading: the writer then writes the offer's lines into it, and the subshell removes its temporary file, writes its refusal to the dead hook's stderr, and exits. Where the signal goes to the process group, nothing survives, and only the temporary file stays. Which signal Claude Code sends is not verified (lane 2's further notes on part 51, NIT-7; :22073–22086). Lane 3 accepts this window as a limit for v1.3, its consequence stated as it is, lane 2's first option (lane 3's rulings, #165 6894116246, point 27's windows). A symbolic link to `/dev/null` put there is no longer read as "made": since T502's part 49, "made" also requires a regular file (`-f`), so that link, though it is the very file the descriptor opened, is a 5, and nothing is removed for it; the offer site then answers it as a record that is there and is not a regular file, "It is not a regular file (a FIFO, a socket or a device, or a link to one): remove it, then send your prompt again." (:22041, :22059–22062, :22105–22106, :22176–22187).
        - **The dangling link's `rm`, from its test to its `rm`** (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, its answer 3; lane 3's rulings, #165 6894714082, which ask it stated under point 27 with its consequence; lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, NIT 5): the offer site tests that the record's path is a link that names nothing, then removes it by an unconditional `rm -f`, and its retry makes the offer (00e2c92e:22171–22175, :22241–22243). A record put in place between the test and the `rm` is removed, while its `.bind.*` files are not, and the retry then makes this prompt's offer in its place, where nothing is there again. That record can be another prompt's offer, made by its own retry after it dropped the same link, and the question has then been asked for each; or, where that offer was also answered `2` in that instant, its `binding`, whose outer then runs nothing, its start rewrite finding no record, or, where its body had started, writes how the bind ends only to its `.out`, which is kept, while the new offer counts its attempts from the first again. Where a record is at the path again before the retry's create, the retry writes nothing, and the prompt is refused with "the pending offer could not be recorded: a record of this transcript's is at `<file>` and could not be replaced, so nothing was written to it. Send your prompt again and the question will be asked afresh.", the record then there being left as it is (:22241–22245).
        - **The nested answer is closed** (part 34): where `guard_answer`, called from `guard_offer_site`, answers 3 and an allowance that fits is there, it passes; any other record meets the retry and is refused, and nothing is lost (00e2c92e:22228–22238).
      - **A platform limit for v1.3, not a window: where `/dev/fd/3` cannot be read** (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, NIT-3; lane 3's rulings, #165 6894714082, in point 26's form; lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, NIT 4). Nothing is built for it in v1.3. **The condition:** hard links are refused in `offers/`, and `/dev/fd/3` cannot be `stat()`ed, as on Linux without `/proc`. **The consequence:** a first mismatch prompt of `1`, `2` or `3` is acted on as an answer to a question never shown. The writer's own complete offer fails the descriptor's test, a 5, nothing is removed for it, and since the record is there, the guard returns 3 and says nothing (00e2c92e:22041, :22053–22086, :22105–22106); the offer site then answers it as a record that is there, a `name-drift` offer, by `guard_answer` with the prompt that triggered it (:22194–22195, :22228–22238). It needs no interleaving and has no width: it happens on every offer made on such a host. Lane 1 reasoned it from bash's source and did not run it.
      - **Codex's case** is in section 8.
    - The rule below is unchanged: `guard_offer`'s write is creation, not a rewrite, and the only creation (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, ruling 5, below; section 10's point 26).
  - **Rewritten** by the hook to `mode=binding`, "by temp file and `mv`" (Y:5393; tasks:602), with the attempt's first keys (section 3).
  - **The hook takes the outer's pid,** the `<p>` its "BINDING" text prints, as `$!` inside Y:5394's fork, returned through a command substitution. `$detach` runs as a bash array, never as a string or through `eval`. Lane 3's probe on Eagle found `$!` equal to the outer's `$$` in all three forms, `setsid`, python3 and `nohup`, and not under `eval` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4).
  - **Rewritten** by the outer at its start, "with its own `$$` and start time" (Y:5396), and at its end, with the outcome (Y:5486–5488), but only over its own `binding` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18):
    - **At its start, only over a record that still says `binding`,** since T502's part 21 (lane 5's posts, #165 6885608994 and 6885751796; lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, which finds that part 21 matches ruling 5 for this rewrite). The start rewrite is `guard_bind_rewrite --binding` (beeae571:22694–22695), which runs only on a record that is there and whose first `mode` line, as `guard_bind_load` takes it, says `binding`, tested on the very lines it copies (beeae571:20473–20492, :20548). Where the record is gone at the rewrite's test that it exists (beeae571:20549–20550) or at its open (:20544), or says any other mode, it writes nothing, removes its temporary file and returns 2 (:20556–20558). The outer then runs nothing and returns 2, as it does where its load finds no record or one not `binding` (beeae571:22645–22652, :22696–22699; section 7). A 1 is as before: the record could not be rewritten, and nothing ran (:22700–22703). Before part 21 this rewrite made no test, so a record gone between the outer's load and the rewrite was made again with the start's keys alone and no `mode`, an offer that had taken the record's place took the start's keys, and in both the bind ran (ae702c74:22626, :22666, :20497; lane 2's answers, #165 6885568373, for the record gone; lane 2's case, #165 6885773752, T214t's `[no-longer-binding-at-its-start]`, for the offer in its place). Its window is the rewrite's own, from its read of the record's lines to its `mv`, as at the outcome (below).
    - Just before the outer writes its outcome, it reads the record again. It writes only where the record is still its own `binding`, with its own `pid` and `pidstart`. Otherwise it leaves the record as it is: case 11 can leave a live outer behind, and by its end the file may hold another offer's record (draft 3's point 18, ruled in section 10).
    - **Its own `pidstart`** is the token the outer wrote at its start, or none where it wrote `pid` alone, its `pid_start_of $$` having exited 2 (section 3's `pid` row; lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15). An outer whose own start could not be read still writes its outcome, as every outer does (Y:5486–5488; svc:148–154). Lane 3 confirmed this reading of its points 15 and 18: "That is how lane 3 meant points 15 and 18, and the code already does it" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 19; code:22529–22530, :22661–22662, :20372; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 1).
    - **Its own host and container:** the test also matches the record's `host` and `container` with the outer's own, `WS` and `LANES_CONTAINER_NAME`, without regard to case, as the guard's site compares them (section 3's grammar; code:22076; lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S2). `WS` is `LANES_WORKSTATION`, else `hostname -s`, else `hostname`, else `unknown` (code:1352–1362, :1970–1971). `LANES_CONTAINER_NAME` is `LANES_CONTAINER`, else, inside a container, the host's probe, else `none` (code:1582–1592, :1995–1997). The rewrite's own test uses the same values and the same case-blind match. In lane 3's words: "A pid and its start time name a process only inside one pid namespace, as the lane protocol's Amendment 18 holds. `out` is not matched." (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 20). While the outer lives, no other process on its host and container has its pid, so with them matched a `binding` record that holds that `pid` is its own attempt's.
    - **So the test is:** `mode=binding`; `host` and `container` the outer's own; `pid` the outer's own `$$`; and `pidstart` the token it wrote, or no `pidstart` where it wrote none.
    - **The code:** at `bc9ed80b` the outer tests `mode`, `pid` and `pidstart` (code:22661–22662), and not `host` and `container`. At `a326c043`, T502's part 7, one function makes the whole test, `guard_bind_own`, comparing `host` and `container` with `WS` and `LANES_CONTAINER_NAME` by `lower_into`, so that it forks no process (a326c043:22555–22561). The outer calls it at its read (a326c043:22759–22760), and the rewrite on the lines it copies (a326c043:20509; lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 20 and its code follow-ups).
    - Its `.out` still carries its outcome, for the removers and for a person reading it.
    - The write is still a temporary file and `mv -f` (below). A change between that read and the `mv` is the one window left, which lane 3's ruling calls "milliseconds wide" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18). Lane 3 keeps it, with no lock per record, and narrows it (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 21; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 2):
      - **What the outcome writes is gathered first:** the outer takes the five copies from `start-record`'s pending records before it reads the record again, never between that read and its write.
      - **The rewrite makes the same test** on the very lines it copies into its temporary file: the first `mode`, `host`, `container`, `pid` and `pidstart` lines. Where they fail it, it removes its temporary file and leaves the record as it is (ae702c74:20531). The outer then treats the record as no longer its own: its outcome goes to its `.out` alone, and it exits 0 (section 7's exits).
      - **The rewrite's three results** (lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S1, which lane 3 takes as written), and the outer's exit for each: 0, written, and the outer exits 0; 1, an I/O failure, and the outer exits 1, its outcome in its `.out` alone; 2, the record not the outer's own, or no record, not written: this rewrite did not change the record; and the outer exits 0, its outcome in its `.out` alone. A 1 is any failure the rewrite can see, since T502's part 16: its `mktemp`, a write to its temporary file, the open of a record that is still there, a `read` that ends with a status other than 1, or its `mv` (ae702c74:20481–20482, :20495, :20519, :20536, :20522, :20528–20529, :20521, :20544; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, finding 1). No record is a record gone before the rewrite's test that it exists (ae702c74:20532–20533), and, since T502's part 17, one gone between that test and its open, which at `4fb88e68` was a 1 and the outer's exit 1 (ae702c74:20522–20530; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, finding 4). After a 1, in lane 3's words, "this rewrite did not change the record; it is as another writer may have left it" (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S1): the failure may come before the rewrite's own test or at its `mv`, and another writer may have changed the record meanwhile. T502's parts 15 and 18 give the code's comments the same sense in their own words, "this rewrite did not change the record, which is as another writer may have left it", and at the outer "and not necessarily still its `binding`" (ae702c74:20471–20472, :22818–22819; lane 4's check, #165 6885294533, N4).
      - **The facts file goes on each of the three** (lane 3's ruling on S1, the lifecycle as built; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S1). Its `why=` and `note=` lines enter the outcome only where the body ended on its own; where the outer's bound or a signal stopped the body, the outer reads nothing the file holds, and the outcome takes Y:5488's `why` (12eddddf:22709–22745; ae702c74:22738–22758; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6885771975, S2). The outcome reaches the record only on the rewrite's 0, and stays in the attempt's `.out` on a 1 or a 2; the file is removed after each of the three. This is the code after T502's round 2 (12eddddf:22783–22810; `9ef79db4`): the facts file is removed after a 2 (12eddddf:22798–22801), a 1 (:22803–22806) and a 0 (:22808–22810). At `bc9ed80b` and `a326c043` the outer left it after a 1 (code:22680–22682; a326c043:22771–22773), and `a326c043` already returns 2 only under `--own` (a326c043:20470–20471, :20509–20519) and maps it so (a326c043:22766–22773).
      - **The window left** is the rewrite's own, from its read of the record's lines to its `mv`, in which a change by another writer can still be overwritten, and a record another writer removes is made again (below) (ae702c74:20466–20470, :22809–22812; T502's part 18; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, finding 8).
      - **The code:** at `bc9ed80b` the outer gathers the five copies after its read (code:22661, :22675–22679), and its rewrite copies the lines without the test (code:20471–20475). At `a326c043`, T502's part 8, the copies are gathered before the read (a326c043:22731–22735, then :22758–22760), and the rewrite's copy loop takes the five lines and makes the test (a326c043:20486–20509; lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 21 and its code follow-ups).
  - **Rewritten** by the guard after a failure, to `mode=name-drift` with `bind=k` (Y:5502–5503), and at the stale row (Y:5505).
  - **Every rewrite,** by the hook, the outer or the guard, writes a temporary file in `offers/` and runs `mv -f` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 2). `guard_offer`'s write of the offer does not: it is creation, and this rule is unchanged (above; lane 3's ruling, #165 6885378657, ruling 4, (a); lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, point 26).
    - **No rewrite creates a record, outside each rewrite's window from its read to its `mv`** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, ruling 5, which takes lane 5's rule, #165 6885751796; the window, lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, SHOULD-1). Every rewrite runs only on a record that is there at its read, and where the record is gone then, it writes nothing. A record removed after that read and before the `mv` is made again by the `mv` (below). Outside that window, `guard_offer`'s write stays the only creation (above).
      - **By T502's part 26, every rewrite keeps it, outside its window:** parts 22, 25 and 26 together completed it, since at part 22, `b3ece59b`, choice 1 still moved its allowance into place whatever was there, and could make a record that had gone (lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S4; 6253b0ed:20490–20657). `guard_bind_rewrite` has four modes and two tests that go with any of them, and each caller names what it requires; where a test fails, the record gone at the test that it exists or at the open among them, it writes nothing and returns 2 (:20625, :20628–20644, :20650–20653). `--own`: the outer's outcome, unchanged (:23356). `--binding`: the outer's start since part 21 (:23187), the hook's back-out since part 22 (:22976), and `guard_bind_site`'s rewrites of cases 9 and 10 since part 30 (:22520, :22582). `--exist`: the record there, whatever its mode, which no caller now names. `--offer`: the record there and its first `mode` line, as `guard_bind_load` takes it, `name-drift`: the hook's copy of the prefetch's keys and its rewrite to `binding` (:20744, :22936). `--mode <m>` and `--match <key>=<value>`, since part 30: the first line of that key, as `guard_bind_load` takes it, is exactly that, checked by parameter expansion and never a here-string since part 35 (:20554–20560, :20631–20642). `guard_bind_site` requires the attempt it loaded: cases 6 and 7 `--mode bind-failed --match bind=<k>`, since parts 30 and 39, and cases 9 and 10 `--match bind=<k>` and `--match started_utc=<t>` (:22424–22430, :22486, :22520, :22582). The hook requires the offer that was answered: `--offer` with `--match` on its `from`, `to`, `session` and `utc`, each where it has one, since part 32 (`guard_answer_match`, :22627–22639, :22724–22725). Choice 1's `mode=allow` record, which is not written through `guard_bind_rewrite`, makes the same test of the answered offer just before its `mv`, by `guard_offer_still`, since part 26, and on the answered offer's identity since part 32 (:22702–22715, :22809), and a record that is there and cannot be read is its I/O failure, since part 34 (:22805–22808). Since T502's part 57, cases 6 and 7 also require the `started_utc` they loaded, by `guard_site_match`, as cases 9 and 10 do; the hook's back-out requires its own `binding` by `--match` on its `bind`, `started_utc` and `out`; and `--exist` is kept only for the proof's `r7-flag-exist-*` cases, as `guard_offer_drop` is for the proof's rounds 7 to 12 (lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, NITs 1, 2 and 6; de96ef86:23033–23041, :23742–23749, :20640–20644, :20444–20448).
      - **At `beeae571`, only the outer's two kept it.** Without a mode, a record gone before the rewrite's test that it exists was made again from the rewrite's own keys, and one gone at its open was a 1 (beeae571:20513, :20544–20545, :20549–20555, :20561). Those were the hook's copy of the prefetch's `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` (beeae571:20647, :22449), its rewrite to `binding` (:22470–22473), its back-out (:22499) and `guard_bind_site`'s rewrites (:22168, :22198, :22256); and choice 1's `mode=allow` write made no test either (beeae571:22363–22375). Lane 5's post named the head copy and choice 1 after draft 17's part 1 (#165 6886054653).
      - **The hook's writes on the offer it answered** (lane 5's rule for them, which lane 3 confirmed: it "matches ruling 5 and point 26 as ruled again", #165 6886113842): the head copy, the rewrite to `binding` and choice 1's write run only on a record that is there, still says `name-drift` and is still the offer that was answered. Where it is not, they write nothing, and the record's mode is read once and answered for, with no step repeated (`guard_answer_moved`, 6253b0ed:22641–22700): gone, or any mode but those below, (A), or for choice 1 its mirror, a re-made `name-drift` offer being "another offer now"; `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed`, `guard_bind_site`'s answer, as the guard's first read gives it; `allow`, 3, the allow branch. Each caller's result is in sections 5 and 7.
      - **The window each test leaves, accepted as a limit for v1.3** (section 10's point 27): from its read to the `mv`, since bash has no rename that replaces only an existing file (6253b0ed:20540, :22790–22792; `guard_bind_rewrite`'s open and `mv` now at 00e2c92e:20713 and :20749, and choice 1's check and `mv` at :23305–23307). A record another writer makes or changes in it is replaced by this writer's, as at the outcome (above). Two consequences more are lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, SHOULD-1, which lane 3's ruling on point 27 does not name, and which lane 3 confirms under it (#165 6894714082):
        - **A record removed in the window is made again.** The `mv` puts the rewrite's record where none is, and the rewrite returns 0, as where the record was there: the hook's rewrite to `binding` writes `mode=binding`, and choice 1 writes `mode=allow` and prints ALLOWED (00e2c92e:20749–20750, :23307, :23358). Lane 1's experiment E3, a test-only copy paused just before the `mv`, showed both, after `guard_drop_if name-drift` removed the offer in the window, as an answer `3` or a stale-identity drop does. Where the hook's rewrite to `binding` makes the record again, the hook goes on to fork the outer and print BINDING, so a bind starts on an offer that another prompt dropped, by an answer `3` that typed `/rename` or by the drop for a stale identity (00e2c92e:23228, :23472–23494, :23551–23553, :23558–23561). And where a removal lands inside an outer's own start or outcome rewrite, that outer's `mv` makes the record again, and the bind runs with its record its own: a third outcome, beside the two the status 4, `guard_allow_drop` and case 11 windows give, that the outer runs nothing or writes its outcome only to its `.out` (lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, NIT 3). So "No rewrite creates a record" holds only outside this window (above).
        - **Two answers `2` to one offer, both inside the window, can both start an outer.** Each takes the files' index before either forks, so both name the same `.bind.<n>.out`, and the second fork's `>` empties the first outer's live `.out` (00e2c92e:23426, :23494). The outer's start rewrite, `--binding`, tests only the mode, and the test that the record already names its outer is made only at the outer's load, so where both outers load before either rewrites, both bodies run (:23690–23701, :23740–23745). The record then names only the outer whose start rewrite's `mv` lands last (:20749). The other outer's `--own` test fails at its outcome, so it writes nothing to the record, notes that the record is no longer its own, and exits 0, its outcome going only to the shared `.out`, which the second fork emptied and both outers write into (:23924–23934). Both outers take the same facts file, from the same `out=` (:23723); each removes it at its start (:23755), and the first to end removes it at its outcome (:23933, :23938, :23941), so one body's `why=` and `note=` can be lost, or folded into the other's outcome. And two `lane-start --no-launch --bind` runs for one transcript then run at once: what they do together is not known here, since neither T502's code nor this contract settles it. Lane 1 reasoned this from the code and did not run it; the consequence after "both bodies run" is lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, SHOULD 2, each part read here against the code at `00e2c92e`, and none run.
        - **The proposal, not built for v1.3,** is a claim by rename: the writer renames the record it read to a private name first, which succeeds only where the record is still there, checks what it claimed, then renames its new record into place. It is point 27's `mkdir` claim in another form (section 10).
      - **Choice 1's window, from its check to its `mv`, not closed, and accepted as a limit for v1.3** (S3; 00e2c92e:23150–23157, :23256–23288, :23305–23307): `guard_offer_still` is one read of the record since part 44, and an answer `2` to the same offer, made at the same moment, can pass its own `--offer` test after that read, rewrite the offer to `binding` and start its outer; the `mv` then replaces that bind record with the allowance. Where that outer's start rewrite comes after the `mv`, it finds no `binding` and runs nothing (part 21). Where its body had started, the bind runs to its end, and its outcome, its record being no longer its own, goes to its `.out` alone, which is kept where its index is not one choice 1 listed: choice 1 sweeps only the offer's own names listed before its check (section 5). The fork makes the `.out` before the outer starts, so choice 1's second listing, after the `mv`, finds it wherever the body can run, and the person is told so in place of ALLOWED (section 5). Where that outer's start rewrite read `binding` before the `mv` and makes its own `mv` after it, that rewrite's window writes the outer's `binding` over the allowance, and the bind runs with its record its own, so three of that note's clauses are false: "The allowance is recorded"; "its outer leaves a record that is no longer its own, so how the bind ends is written only to that output", since its outcome reaches the record (00e2c92e:23740–23749, :23924–23927); and "if its outer had not yet made its start rewrite, it runs nothing" (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, NIT-1, which lane 3's ruling on point 27 does not name, confirmed under it by lane 3, #165 6894714082, a reading lane 3 confirms in its review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e`, spec#65 6898800291, call (a); lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, NIT 2). The window that answer `2` renamed to `<to>` stays so named, and the next prompt judges the allowance against the names as they are then: it holds only while the lane and the session name it was made for are unchanged (00e2c92e:22251–22258). Under a reused index the consequence is worse (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, SHOULD-2). The files' index is one above the highest present (00e2c92e:22475–22493), so after a full sweep the next attempt takes index 1 again, and a name listed before the check can then name a newer attempt's file. Where choice 1 listed `.bind.1.out` before its check, a name at or below the offer's own index wherever the offer names an attempt, and between its check and its `mv` another prompt drops that offer with its `.bind.*` files, by the stale-identity drop or choice 3's, a new offer is made, and an answer `2` to it takes index 1 again, rewrites it to `binding` and starts its outer: choice 1 then writes its allowance over that `binding`, sweeps `.bind.1.out`, which is now that bind's live output, as the offer's own, and, since index 1 is not above the offer's own, its second listing does not report it, and the person is told ALLOWED for an offer that is no longer there (00e2c92e:23256–23288, :23290, :23305–23307, :23319–23358). That outer then runs nothing, its start rewrite finding no `binding`, or, where its body had started, its outcome is written to the unlinked `.out` and lost. Where `.bind.1.out` was not listed, that `.out` is kept, and how the bind ends is written there; but the second listing reports only names above the offer's own and not listed, and the offer's own is at least 1 wherever the offer named an attempt or a listed name is there again at the sweep, so there too the person is told ALLOWED for an offer that is no longer there (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 48 to 50, its held item (B), as part 51's comment states it; :23267–23285, :23319–23348). The ways to close the window, none of them built for v1.3, are in section 10's point 27.
    - **The temporary file** is new: `mktemp` makes it in `offers/`, beside the record, under `umask 077`, with a name no file has, created only where none exists, and 0600 from its creation. No rewrite writes to a fixed or reused path (code:20465; choice 1's `mode=allow` record, code:22246).
    - **Where a write to the temporary file, the record's open or the `mv` fails,** nothing is moved into place, and the temporary file is removed as best effort: its `rm -f` is not checked, so where the directory or its file system fails after `mktemp`, the removal can fail too and leave that 0600 file in `offers/` (ae702c74:20539–20544, :22354–22358, which are beeae571:20556–20561, :22371–22375; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6885771975, S4, lane 5's choice of its two, with no code change). This rewrite did not change the record, which is as another writer may have left it (lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 2; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, findings 1 and 8). So it is for `guard_bind_rewrite`, whose every write and whose open of the record are checked since T502's part 16 (ae702c74:20484–20545), and for choice 1's `mode=allow` record, whose six writes are each checked since T502's part 20, a failure being the guard's refusal with 2 and no `mv` (ae702c74:22346–22358). A read error inside a record that opened is the one failure the shell cannot see, since `read` answers 1 at the end of the file and on an error alike (ae702c74:20490–20493). Before parts 16 and 20 a failed early write was hidden by a later one that worked, and the record was moved into place without that line (code:20467–20484, :22247–22254; a6ee7c0b:22340–22347).
  - **Removed** by the guard after "MOVED" (Y:5501), and dropped by it where it cannot be read (Y:5507). Removal is `rm` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 2).
    - **A drop that follows other work reads the mode again,** since T502's part 27 (lane 5's adversarial review of parts 22 to 26, F1; 6253b0ed:20373–20407). `guard_run` reads the mode once, before its clauses (d) and (e) and its table, and a drop made on that read removed a bind record that another invocation had made since, with its live outer's `.out`. So `guard_answer`'s drop of a stale offer, and choice 3's after `/rename` is typed, go through `guard_drop_if`: it lists the record's `.bind.*` names, then reads the first `mode` line again, and removes the record and only the names it listed, only while it still says `name-drift` (:20385–20399, :22766, :23009; part 41). The drops of an allowance, in the row where the three names agree and in the offer site's allow branch, go through `guard_allow_drop`, which removes the record only while it still says `allow` (:20400–20407, :21714, :22041). `guard_bind_site`'s drop of a `bound` record, case 5, goes through `guard_drop_if` too since T502's part 43, with `all` and the `bind`, `started_utc`, `pid` and `ended_utc` it loaded: the `.bind.*` names are listed before its read, and the record and those names go only where it is still that `bound`, the mode and each value compared on one read by `guard_record_says`; where it is not, nothing is removed, and `guard_site_moved` answers the record once (00e2c92e:20377–20441, :20468–20488, :22821–22853; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S2).
    - **`guard_allow_drop`'s exception to point 13's sweep:** it removes the record alone, with no `.bind.*` sweep, since an `allow` record owns no attempt: choice 1 removes the offer's own `.bind.*` files as it writes the allowance, since T502's parts 44 and 47 only those listed before its check at or below the offer's own index (6253b0ed:20400–20407; 00e2c92e:23319–23337; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 13; section 5). It is lane 5's reviewer's finding G, which needs no code change.
    - **`guard_allow_drop`'s window, accepted as a limit for v1.3** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, point 26, which asks every remaining window stated with its consequence; section 10's point 27): in its milliseconds from its read to its `rm`, it can remove a bind record another invocation made in between, though never that bind's `.out`, since it sweeps no `.bind.*` (6253b0ed:20400–20404). The live outer's `--own` outcome then goes only to its `.out`, since `--own` finds no record (section 7), unless the `rm` lands inside that outer's own start or outcome rewrite, whose `mv` makes the record again (section 1), and the next prompt finds no record and is asked the question again, while that bind may still be running.
    - **`guard_drop_if`'s window, accepted as a limit for v1.3:** from its read to its `rm`, a record put in its place is still removed, with the `.bind.*` names listed before the read, while a `.bind.*` file made after the listing, a fresh `.out` among them, survives, unless it reuses a listed name, as under case 5's window below (6253b0ed:20387–20396). Where the record so removed is a new `binding` whose outer has not yet started, part 21's start finds no record and runs nothing (section 7's outer 2).
    - **Case 5's window, from its drop's read to its `rm`, accepted as a limit for v1.3** (S2): a record put in place between them is removed, with the `.bind.*` names listed before the read. Outside each rewrite's window, no rewrite turns a `bound` into another record in one step (inside it, the `mv` replaces what is there, section 1), so the record removed there can only be one that another prompt made after dropping this `bound`: a new offer, which the next prompt makes again, or, where that offer was also answered `2`, all between this read and this `rm`, its `binding`. And the files' index is one above the highest present (00e2c92e:22475–22493), so where the other prompt dropped this `bound` with its files, that answer `2` takes index 1 again, and where `.bind.1.out` was among the names listed before the read, that name is now its `.out`: the drop then removes that `binding` and its live `.out` with it (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, SHOULD-1). That newer attempt is lost: its outer runs nothing, its start rewrite finding no record (part 21), or, where its body had started, the bind runs on, and how it ends is written only to the unlinked `.out` and is lost. Where `.bind.1.out` was not listed, the `binding` is still removed, but its `.out` is kept, and how the bind ends is written there. Either way the prompt that dropped the record still says MOVED (00e2c92e:20468–20488, :22821–22864, the window in part 51's words at :22829–22849; lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 48 to 50, its held item (A)).
    - **The facts file's name under a reused index, accepted as a limit for v1.3** (lane 3's rulings, #165 6894116246, point 27's windows, which names it; lane 5's check of T502's part 51, NIT-1; not new in part 51): an outer reads its facts file, and removes it at its start and at each outcome, by the name it derived from the record's `out=` at its start, and its body removes that same name where its account could not be written: a name, not a file (00e2c92e:23723, :23755, :23841–23849, :23933, :23938, :23941, :24331). Where case 11 drops a live outer's record, a later removal of the record's `.bind.*` names sweeps that attempt's files, and after a full sweep a new offer's answer `2` takes index 1 again (00e2c92e:22475–22493): where the live attempt's index is 1, the newer attempt's body makes and writes its account under that very name, and the two attempts share one facts file, the later write being what is there. Where the older body then ends on its own with the newer account there, its outer folds the newer attempt's `why=` and `note=` lines into its own outcome, which goes only to its own `.out`, unlinked by that sweep, since the record is no longer its own (`--own`'s 2), and removes the file at its outcome; the newer attempt's outer, reading after that, finds no facts file, and its outcome loses those lines and says that the body gave no account (point 16(d)) or no reason (point 16(c)) (00e2c92e:23822–23839, the window in part 52's words; :23851–23868, :23924–23934).
    - **Case 11's drops, on a read apart from the one acted on, as at `00e2c92e`, and fixed by T502's part 55** (lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, SHOULD-2, which lane 3's ruling on point 27 does not name, and which lane 3 confirms under it, #165 6894714082; older than T502's part 42, at 6253b0ed:22460 and :22749): `guard_bind_site` drops a record that `guard_bind_load` cannot load or `guard_bind_readable` refuses, and `guard_answer` one whose `mode` is not `name-drift`, or with no `from`, or with a `bind` that is not 1 to 3, each by an unconditional `rm -f` (00e2c92e:22806–22811, :23180–23213). Each read is apart from the one its caller acted on: `guard_bind_site` is called on a mode read earlier by a separate `sed` (:21632, :22194, :22660, :23062), and `guard_answer` reads the record by separate `sed` calls (:23182–23194). So a record that is readable, and has become another one since that read, is dropped as unreadable: an offer that a case 6 restored meanwhile, so that the next offer counts its attempts from the first again; or a live `binding`, whose `.out` is left, as case 11 leaves it, its outer running nothing or, where its body had started, writing how the bind ends to that `.out` alone, or, where the drop lands inside that outer's own start or outcome rewrite, its `mv` making the record again (section 1). Lane 1's experiment E4 showed both: two prompts read `bind-failed`, the first's case 6 restored the offer and said KEPT, and the second dropped that offer with "the pending offer at `<file>` could not be read, so it has been dropped rather than answered on a guess."; and `guard_answer`, run on a live `binding`, removed it and left its `.out`. Lane 3 ruled it a fix, not a limit (lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, #165 6894385553), and T502's part 55, `6e37a188`, builds it. `guard_answer` sends a `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed` record to `guard_bind_site`, and returns 3 for an allowance, before its drop, so neither is dropped as an offer it cannot read (de96ef86:23399–23422). `guard_bind_site`'s drop rests on its load's one read: an offer or an allowance read there is not unreadable, nothing is removed, and the prompt is refused with "the pending offer at `<file>` was changed while it was being answered (it says mode=`<m>` now), so nothing was written to it. Send your prompt again and it will be read afresh." (:22912–22948). Lane 1's check of part 55 finds that it settles both halves, for a record that can be read when it is read, E4 run again: a live `binding` is answered as BINDING IN PROGRESS and kept with its `.out`, and the restored offer is kept (#165 6895648101). Since T502's part 58, `3ce9eb58`, a record gone by the drop is neither read nor dropped: an existence test just before the `rm` sends it to `guard_bind_gone`'s words, "the pending offer at `<file>` was gone when it came to be answered, so nothing was written to it. Send your prompt again and it will be read afresh." (lane 1's check of part 55, NIT 1; :22718–22721, :22950–22965, :23424–23429). **The window left,** stated in part 58's comments with its cost (NIT 2): from `guard_bind_site`'s load, and from `guard_answer`'s separate reads, to the `rm` (de96ef86:22922–22925, :23407–23412). A record replaced there by an `mv`, or removed and made again before the existence test, is removed. The test gives only a record still gone at that instant `guard_bind_gone`'s words, and that record is not removed; the test does not narrow the window, since a record put in place after the read or reads and before the test is still removed, as lane 1 showed in both functions (lane 1's check of parts 58 and 59, #165 6897192211, NIT-1, which corrects that comment's last sentence and lane 1's own part-55 NIT 1). A record put in place there is removed; where it is another prompt's live `binding`, its outer then runs nothing, its start rewrite finding no record, or, where its body had started, the bind runs on and how it ends is written only to its `.out`, which case 11 never removes (:22913–22936, :23399–23416). It is a window of point 27's kind, and the proposal of a drop made only on a fresh read that still fails is not built. Since T502's part 61, `475a04a4`, those comments say so too: a record gone by the existence test gets `guard_bind_gone`'s words and is not removed, and the test does not narrow the window (lane 1's check of parts 58 and 59, NIT-1; 475a04a4:22977–22981, :23487–23491).
- **Its mode bits:** every write is under `umask 077`, so the record is 0600 and a new `offers/` is 0700. An existing `offers/` is not `chmod`'d (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 1; after Y:5172's "mode 0600, in a 0700 directory" for F10's pending record, and Y:5394's and Y:5408's fork).
- **The per-attempt output file,** `<offers>/<uuid>.bind.<k>.out`, `<k>` being the files' index, which is apart from the attempt (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233):
  - **The files' index** is one above the highest index among the uuid's `.bind.*` files, and 1 where there are none. The hook names it at the fork (Y:5394). So a new attempt never reuses a `<k>` whose files are still there, and Y:5394's `>` never meets a live outer's `.out` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18, as lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233, corrects it). That holds outside the rewrite's window: where two answers `2` to one offer both fall inside it, both take the same index before either forks, and the second fork's `>` empties the first outer's live `.out`; both outers then write into that one `.out`, the record names only one of them, and the two share one facts file, as section 1 states (section 1; lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, SHOULD-1, reasoned from the code and not run; lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, SHOULD 2). Every canonical index counts, `[1-9][0-9]*` as section 3's `out` row gives it, of the ten ASCII digits and matched against the whole index (ae702c74:21999–22002, T502's part 19), compared as a number whatever its length, so 1000000 is above 999999 and the claim holds past six digits (lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S4, lane 5's choice of its two). This is the code after T502's round 2; at `bc9ed80b` an index over six digits is passed over (code:21900). T502's part 57 states the index's reuse after a full sweep in its comment, as point 27's window: a removal that listed `.bind.1.*` before the sweep, or an old outer's removal of its own facts file, can meet a newer attempt's live `.bind.1.out` or `.bind.1.facts`; an index that never repeats would close it, and is not built (lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, NIT 5; de96ef86:22543–22557).
  - **It is not the attempt.** `bind` stays the attempt count, 1 to 3 per offer (Y:5429), and `bind=k+1` at the next `2` counts attempts only (section 3). The `k` of `bind=k` is the attempt, and the `<k>` of the files' names is the index: two numbers. After a case 11, the next offer's attempts count from 1 again, while their files' index runs on above the leftover files' (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233).
  - The outer's stdout and stderr go to it, with its children's (Y:5394, Y:5408).
  - It is 0600: the hook forks under `( umask 077; … )` (Y:5394), and Y:5408 has "stdout and stderr to one 0600 file per attempt" (also Ytp:5970).
  - The record's `out` names it (Y:5431): the attempt's own file. The outer and the guard find it there, not by `bind`, and the facts file sits beside it (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233). It is kept after a failure (Y:5487). The outer, the body and the guard's sweep all find the facts file from `out=` too, never from `bind` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118; below).
- **The per-attempt facts file,** `<offers>/<uuid>.bind.<k>.facts`, 0600, beside the `.out` that the record's `out` names, with the same `<k>`: it is `out=`'s `<offers>/<uuid>.bind.<k>` stem with `.facts` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 6; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233; lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118):
  - **The outer, the body and the guard's sweep** all find it from `out=`, never from `bind`. Where the body cannot read the record itself, the outer hands it that path (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118).
  - The body creates it, empty, before its first step. A missing file then means the body never ran (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(a)).
  - The body writes `why=` and `note=` to it.
  - The outer folds them into its outcome where the body ended on its own. Where its bound or a signal stopped the body, the outcome takes Y:5488's `why`, and the outer reads nothing the file holds (12eddddf:22709–22745). The outcome reaches the record only on its rewrite's 0; on a 1 or a 2, and where its read finds the record no longer its own, it stays in the attempt's `.out` alone. On each of those results the outer then removes the file, whether or not the outcome is written (above; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S1; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6882105713, S1; 12eddddf:22790–22810).
  - Which end is the body's own, and what the outer writes where the body gave no reason or left no file, are section 3's `why` and `note=` rows (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16).
- **Their lifetime:** the `.bind.*` files and the facts file live as long as the bind. The guard removes them wherever it removes the record or rewrites it to `mode=allow` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 13). Case 11 is one exception, below, and `guard_allow_drop`, which removes an allowance that owns no `.bind.*` files, the other (above).
  - **A facts file** lives from its body's start until its outer reaches its outcome and removes it, whether the outcome is written or not (above): a normal one ends with its attempt, and only an outer that never reaches its outcome leaves one (below).
  - **An `.out` file** is kept after a failure (Y:5487), until that removal.
  - **So an offer holds** at most three `.out` files, one per attempt (Y:5504), and the running attempt's one `.facts`.
  - **Besides them,** the uuid may hold leftovers until the next removal sweeps `<uuid>.bind.*`: a facts file an outer did not remove, as after a `kill -9`, case 9 or case 10, and, after a case 11, an earlier offer's files (lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 9).
  - **The offer site's sweep of stale temporaries** (T514's parts 64 and 65; lane 3's rulings on lane 5's question 3, #165 6932653746, on the sweep's race, #165 6934144108, and on the Codex review of spec#81 at `18e537ad`, #165 6934311646): before its create, the offer site removes each regular file in `offers/` named `.<uuid>.` and six characters, this transcript's and this user's, whose mtime is more than 10 minutes old: the leftover of a writer stopped between its `mktemp` and its `mv` or `rm`, the FIFO window above among them. It sweeps only where `offers/` is a directory, no symbolic link, this user's, and writable by no group or other, and sweeps nothing where it cannot list `offers/` or read its mode. The sweep removes, by name, only a file it found to be a regular file of this user's, named `.<uuid>.` and six characters, more than ten minutes old, in an `offers/` that only this user can write; another process of this user can put a different file at that name between the check and the `rm`, and the `rm` then removes that file instead, a race stated as a limit for v1.3, since such a process can already remove any file there. Removal is best effort: a failed removal leaves the entry, and the later candidates are still considered; a file it cannot date is kept, and where the clock cannot be read the sweep stops and removes nothing more. It removes no symbolic link, directory, FIFO, socket or device, nor any other transcript's file; a hard link of that name is a regular file, and its removal removes that one name only. The offer's creation, its output and its exit are unchanged (the Codex review of spec#81 at `18e537ad`, SHOULD 1). A rewrite stopped for longer than 10 minutes between its `mktemp` and its `mv` loses its temporary, and its `mv` is an I/O failure, a 1: a limit for v1.3, in point 26's form.
  - **The guard's removal also takes any facts file an outer left, as after case 9** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, points 6 and 13). The two rulings are halves of one rule: the outer removes the facts file after every result of its rewrite, having folded it into its outcome only where the body ended on its own and read none of it after a stop by the bound or a signal (point 6; section 1; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S5; 6253b0ed:23265–23278, :23347–23370), and the guard's sweep of `<uuid>.bind.*` (point 13) takes whatever an outer left, as after a `kill -9`, case 9 or case 10.
  - **Outside the windows section 1 states, case 11** is the one removal that drops a record whose outer may still be live while it leaves that attempt's files: it drops the record but leaves `<uuid>.bind.*` for the next removal to sweep, and never unlinks a live outer's `.out` itself (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, points 6 and 13). In those windows, other removals do the same: status 4's `rm` (00e2c92e:22087–22092); choice 1's `mv` over a live `binding`, which keeps its `.out` (:23305–23307, :23332–23337); `guard_allow_drop`'s `rm`, never that bind's `.out` (:20483, :20494–20496); `guard_drop_if`'s, after which a fresh `.out` survives (:20485); and case 5's, which keeps an unlisted `.out` (:22850); and the dangling link's `rm`, which can remove another prompt's `binding` and keeps its `.out` (:22171–22175) (lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, SHOULD 1; lane 3's rulings, #165 6894714082). Every other removal of the record's `.bind.*` names takes only names listed before its read or check: `guard_drop_if`'s, for case 5 since T502's part 43, and for a stale offer and choice 3, and choice 1's sweep, since parts 44 and 47 only the offer's own. So a newer attempt's `.out` made after that listing is not unlinked by them, unless it reuses a listed name: the files' index is one above the highest present, so an attempt made after a full sweep takes index 1 again, and where `.bind.1.out` was listed, that removal can unlink that attempt's live `.out` in its window, as section 1 states (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 48 to 50, its held item (D), and lane 5's check of T502's part 51, NIT-1, as part 52's comment states it, 00e2c92e:20360–20375; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S2 and S3; lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, SHOULD-1 and SHOULD-2). Besides those, an outer removes only its own facts file, by the name it derived from the record's `out=` at its start, at that start and at each outcome, and its body removes that same name where its account could not be written: under a reused index that name can be a newer attempt's, as the facts file's window in section 1 states.
    - The next offer's files take indexes above the leftovers', so none of its attempts reuses them, and the removers (points 6 and 13) still clear the old files as they do now (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233).
    - A live outer left behind writes its outcome only over its own `binding` (above; lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18).

## 2. Its four modes

| Mode | What it says | Written by | Moves to |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode=name-drift` | the offer, waiting for an answer: today's mode, and the mode "once the offer is restored" after an attempt (Y:5427) | the guard: at the offer (pin:10963); after a failure, with `bind=k` and the window renamed back to `<from>` (Y:5502); after the third failure, with `bind=3` (Y:5503); at the stale row, with `rc=unknown` (Y:5505). After the third failure and at the stale row the window is also renamed back to `<from>`, but not for a record from another host or container (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 7; ERR-23), save that since T502's part 62 case 10 renames back a window this answer renamed, with the exceptions in section 5 (#165 6898801339; section 5, case 10's row) | `mode=binding`, by the hook, at an answer `2` while choice 2 is still offered |
| `mode=binding` | an attempt is running, or has not yet written its outcome | the hook, by temp file and `mv` (Y:5393), with the attempt's first keys; the outer rewrites it at its start with the mode unchanged (Y:5396) | `mode=bound` or `mode=bind-failed`, by the outer, only where the record is still its own `binding` when it reads it again (section 1; lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18); `mode=name-drift`, by the guard at the stale row (Y:5505), and for a record from another host or container only after `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s (Y:5506). A record without a well-formed `pid` and `pidstart`, an 11-digit `pid` included, reaches the stale row once `deadline_epoch` has passed (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4; lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15; lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 17) |
| `mode=bound` | the attempt ended 0: `rc=0`, with a `note=` for each act still owed (Y:5486), and one naming `<out>` where the body left no facts file (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(d)) | the outer | removed by the guard, which says "MOVED" once (Y:5501) |
| `mode=bind-failed` | the attempt ended non-0: `lane-start`'s code with its `why` (Y:5487), or 124, 137 or the signal's code where its bound or a signal stopped it (Y:5488). Which end was a stop is section 3's `why` row (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(b)) | the outer | `mode=name-drift`, by the guard at the next prompt, which renames the window back to `<from>` (Y:5502; after the third failure too, by lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 7, ERR-23) |

- **The offer site's branch** takes `binding`, `bound` and `bind-failed`, and `guard_answer` is never called on them (Y:5493; tasks:588). A `mode=name-drift` record goes to `guard_answer`, as today.
- **Outside the bind,** today's file also takes `mode=allow`, choice 1's record (pin:11115), which today's offer site passes by (pin:10651). The bind adds `binding`, `bound` and `bind-failed` to `name-drift` and `allow`.
- **At most three attempts per offer:** "never by itself: a failed bind spends the person's yes; each `2` is one attempt, at most 3 per offer" (Y:5504). After the third failure choice 2 is no longer offered (Y:5503). The files' index, apart from the attempt, leaves this unchanged (Y:5503–5504; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233).
- **A bind stopped by its bound or by a signal** ends `mode=bind-failed`, as every end but 0 does (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 3; Y:5487–5488; svc:152–154; spec:318).

## 3. Every key, with its phase and its writer

Phase 1 is T502's and T514's (T502's parts 8 to 63), with T507's `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms`. Phase 2 is T510's (Y:5558–5559; tasks:569, :1215).

- **The writers Y does not name** are lane 5's reading in draft 1, which lane 3 confirmed with its point 4's change (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 3):
  - The hook writes `bind`, `out`, `host`, `container` and `detach`, and writes the timing keys first. The outer rewrites the timing keys at its start (Y:5436).
  - The hook's `mode=binding` rewrite writes `host`, `container`, `bound_s`, `bounded`, `started_utc` and `deadline_epoch`, and the outer then rewrites the timing keys and adds `pid` and `pidstart` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4). This contract reads the timing keys as those four besides `host` and `container`; lane 3's analysis names `started_utc` and `deadline_epoch` among them.
  - Only the outer writes `pid` and `pidstart`.
- **The hook writes `detach=setsid|python3|nohup`,** the form `$detach` took at the fork (Y:5395; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 8).

| Key | What it holds | Phase, task | Writer |
|---|---|---|---|

| `from`, `to`, `session`, `uuid`, `window`, `pane` | "the offer's identity, as today's file has it" (Y:5428): the lane the window left, the lane named, the session's name, its uuid, the window's reference and its pane (pin:10964–10970) | today's | the guard, at the offer (`guard_offer`, pin:10963–10970); every rewrite keeps them |

Y's key table leaves it out, though Y:5428's identity is "as today's file has it". Nothing reads it: today's reads of the file, at pin:10650, pin:11005–11008 and pin:11078–11081, take other keys | today's | the guard, at the offer; every rewrite keeps it, as part of the identity (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 12) |
| `bind` | "the attempt, 1 to 3" (Y:5429), per offer. It is not the files' index: the `<k>` that the hook names at the fork (Y:5394) is apart from it (section 1; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233) | 1, T502 | the hook, which sets `bind=k+1` at the next `2`, counting attempts only (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, points 3 and 5; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233); the guard keeps it as `bind=k` after a failure (Y:5502–5503). Case 11's check of `bind` is unchanged (section 5) |

| `bound_s` | the bound, "computed at the start by the sum" (Y:5429; section 4) | 1, T502; T507 makes the sum read `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` (tasks:616) | the hook first, in its `mode=binding` rewrite, by section 4's helper (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4; tasks:616); the outer rewrites it at its start: "`guard-bind` computes `bound_s` at the start" (Y:5436) |

The hook's `mode=binding` record has neither until then (section 5). The outer writes `pidstart` only when `pid_start_of $$` exits 0. When it exits 2, the outer writes `pid` alone (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15). A `binding` record without a well-formed `pid` and `pidstart` reads as one with no `pid` (section 5), and so does one where the outer wrote `pid` alone, with `pidstart` empty (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, reading 1). An outer that wrote `pid` alone still writes its outcome: its test of its own `binding` takes no `pidstart` (section 1) |
| `host`, `container` | "`LANES_WORKSTATION` (else the hostname) and `LANES_CONTAINER`" (Y:5430) | 1, T502 | the hook, in its `mode=binding` rewrite (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, points 3 and 4) |

It names the attempt's own file: the outer and the guard find it there, and the facts file sits beside it (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233) | 1, T502 | the hook, which names `<k>` and opens the file at the fork (Y:5394; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 3; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233) |

None where that record holds no `call` line or there is none (Y:5431), and none for a record from another host or container (Y:5506; tasks:1223). The record drops them on LR007 `another-write` (Y:5518), only where `guard_bind_settle` removes `start-record`'s pending record; where the settle fails, they stay (below; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S3). A new attempt keeps them. Where no pending record still holds their `write_id`, the body makes no lookup, and they go at the outcome (Y:5511; tasks:1430 at `f0c7d096`); otherwise they go where the outcome of the body's lookup removed `start-record`'s pending record, and stay where it kept it (below; lane 3's ruling, #165 6841918192, point 10) |

The signal's code is `wait`'s status, 128 plus the signal's number: lane 3's probe gave 143 for `TERM`, and 137 where the body ignores `TERM` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 11). Phase 2 adds `3` and `2` from the lookup (Y:5517–5519) and `start-record`'s codes (Y:5541–5544) | 1, T502; phase 2's codes, T510 | the outer; the guard writes `rc=unknown` at the stale row (Y:5505) |

It says "stopped by a signal" whenever the outer's trap fired (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 11). "Stopped by its bound" needs `timeout`'s 124 or 137 at or after `bound_s` − 10 s from `started_utc`, with the trap not fired. Every other end is the body's own (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(b), which refines point 11: the code alone cannot tell them apart, since a `KILL` from another process inside the bound is 137 too, and a crash is 139). Where the body ended on its own, with a nonzero `rc` and no `why=` line, `why` is "the bind's body ended with exit `<rc>` and gave no reason; what it wrote is not known; its output is `<out>`" (point 16(c)): Y:5487's refusal line may never be printed by a crash, and Y:5488's texts would be false | 1, T502 | the outer: from the body's facts file where the body ended on its own, else Y:5488's text (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 6); point 16(c)'s text where the body ended on its own and gave no `why=` line, a missing facts file included. In phase 2 the five copies still follow Y:5431, an outcome other than 0 (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(c)) |
