In phase 2 any code but 0 from the lane left's `set-row-state` becomes a `note=` with its command, which carries `LANES_LANE=<from>`; the body still exits 0 (tasks:1438 at `f0c7d096`; lane 3's ruling, #165 6841918192, points 4(b) and 7). Where `rc` is 0 and there is no facts file, the record ends `bound` with one `note=` naming `<out>`: a missing file never passes as "nothing owed" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(d)) | 1, T502; T510 | the outer, which folds in the body's facts file only where the body ended on its own, and reads none of it after a stop by the bound or a signal (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 6; section 1; lane 3's Codex delta review of draft 17, S5; 6253b0ed:23265–23278). The body writes no key of the record (Y:5396–5398) |
| `ended_utc` | the outcome's time (Y:5432); at the stale row, "the guard's time" (Y:5505) | 1, T502 | the outer; the guard, at the stale row |

- **The record's grammar,** as every reader takes it (lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 3). Where a form or a fallback below is not Y's or a ruling's, it is T502's or T510's code as built, cited as `code:<n>`:
  - **A line** is `key=value`, split at its first `=`, and ends at a line feed. Every reader passes over a line with no `=` (code:20380–20381).
  - **A key given twice:** the first line wins, for every reader (code:20387–20388; the offer site's and `guard_answer`'s `sed … | head -n1`, code:21349, :22192–22200; section 4's helper, code:20533–20535). Only `note=` repeats, and its lines are kept in their order (code:20383–20385).
  - **An empty value** is read as the table's last column reads a malformed one. For most keys that is the key absent, and `pidstart=` reads as no `pidstart` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, reading 1). Two differ: in `name-drift`, `bind=` drops the offer, where no `bind` counts no attempt spent (code:22202); and at the outer's start any `pid` line, `pid=` included, is an outer before it (code:22511; section 7's exits).
  - **A key no reader knows** is passed over by every reader. Every rewrite keeps it, as it keeps every line it neither drops nor writes (below; code:20471–20475). Only choice 1's `mode=allow` record is written whole.
  - **A value is one line.** The outer makes `why` and each `note=` one line, its control characters spaces, and cuts it at 400 characters, ending " ..." (code:20491–20494).
  - **`mode=allow`** is choice 1's record: `mode=allow`, `from`, `to`, `session`, `uuid` and `utc` (pin:11115–11120; code:22248–22253). It is not a bind mode: the offer site passes it by (pin:10651; code:21358), and the bind writes none of its keys.
  - **Any other `mode`,** or none, goes to `guard_answer`, which drops the record with case 11's text, as today's code does for an offer it cannot read (code:21349–21364, :22218–22221).
  - **Mode by mode,** each key's form, whether it is there, and what its reader does where it is missing or malformed. "Required" is case 11's set (section 5); "kept" is a line an earlier rewrite left, which no reader of that mode reads:

| Key | Its form | `name-drift` | `binding` | `bound`, `bind-failed` | Missing or malformed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | `name-drift`, `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed`; `allow` outside the bind | required | required | required | case 11 in a bind mode; otherwise the bullet above |
| `from`, `to` | lane names | `from` required; `to` empty where the session's name gives no lane, and choice 2 is then unavailable (code:21699, :22264–22267) | required | required | case 11 in a bind mode; in `name-drift`, a missing `from` drops the offer with case 11's text (code:22218) |
| `session`, `uuid`, `window`, `pane` | today's | written at the offer | kept | kept | not tested; `session` may be empty, and `guard_answer` compares it with the session's name (code:22235) |
| `utc` | `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` | written at the offer | kept | kept | never read |
| `bind` | `1`, `2` or `3`, exactly | none before the first `2`; after it, the attempts spent | required | required | case 11 in a bind mode; in `name-drift`, none counts no attempt spent, and any other value drops the offer with case 11's text (code:22200–22204, :22218) |
| `started_utc`, `ended_utc` | `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`, UTC to the second (code:7489–7492, :22351, :22541) | kept, and `ended_utc` written by the stale row | `started_utc` written; `ended_utc` kept from the attempt before | both | not tested. No case decides by them: the guard's clock test is `deadline_epoch`, and it prints "a time the record does not give" in place of one that is missing (section 5). The outer's point 16(b) test reads its own clock at the moment it writes `started_utc` (code:22541–22543, :22603–22604). T510's `w₀` waits `C` from now where `ended_utc` is not of this form (code:22783–22785) |
| `bound_s` | whole seconds | kept | written | kept | only printed: case 3 says "its" bound where it is missing (code:22041) |
| `bounded` | `yes` or `no` | kept | written | kept | any value but `no` reads as `yes` (code:22167, :22180) |
| `deadline_epoch` | seconds since the epoch: digits, at most 12 | kept | required | required | case 11 (code:20437–20438) |
| `pid`, `pidstart` | section 5's well-formed rule | kept | the outer's, from its start | kept | never case 11: section 5's record without a well-formed `pid` and `pidstart` |
| `host`, `container` | as the key table gives: the hook writes its `WS` and `LANES_CONTAINER_NAME` (code:22355; section 1 gives their fallbacks) | kept | required | required | case 11; compared with this guard's own `WS` and `LANES_CONTAINER_NAME` without regard to case (code:22076), and so by the outer's test (section 1) |
| `out` | `<record>.bind.<n>.out`: this record's own path, then the files' index `<n>`, which the hook writes as a whole number above 0 with no leading 0 (section 1; code:21894–21903, :22329) | kept | written | kept | not case 11. A reader takes the facts file only from an `out` that is this record's path, `.bind.`, digits and `.out`, and any other `out` names none: nothing is read from, written to or removed at a facts path made from it (code:21910–21917, :22889–22891). The body reads its own words back from `out` only where `out` has that form too (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 25). One predicate is that form, at the facts file's derivation and at all three read-backs: this record's path, `.bind.`, an index of the ASCII digits 0 to 9 alone, not beginning with 0, and `.out`, so `.bind.0.out` and `.bind.01.out` name nothing (lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S3). `[1-9][0-9]*` is that index as a regular expression matched against the whole index, never a shell pattern, in which `*` would take any string. `guard_bind_index_ok` spells the ten digits out, so no locale's range widens them (ae702c74:21999–22002, T502's part 19; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, finding 10). This is the code after T502's round 2. At `bc9ed80b` the facts file's test takes any digits, those two among them (code:21913–21915), and the body reads its words back from any file `out` names (code:22820–22830, :22921–22937, :23006–23020) |
| `detach` | `setsid`, `python3` or `nohup` | kept | written | kept | any other value, or none, has no session test (section 6; code:22118–22129) |
| `c`, `n_a`, `w_s_ms` | whole numbers | where a `2` copied them | kept | kept | section 4's defaults |
| `act` | T410's `<act-id>@<n>:<generation>`, written only where it parses (code:20593) | where the offer's read was SQL | kept | kept | none: the lane-left call sends no `LANES_ACT` (the `act` row) |
| the five copies | `write_id` and `generation` lower-case UUIDs, `not_after` whole seconds, `write_lane` not empty, `args_sha256` 64 lower-case hex digits (lane 4's corrections, #165 6880140531, C3) | kept, or the stale row's | kept | written at an outcome other than 0 | a `write_id` that is not a UUID makes no lookup, as one no pending record still holds; where one still holds it and another copy is malformed, the body ends 3 and keeps that pending record (code:22764–22773) |
| `rc` | decimal | `unknown` after the stale row; else kept from a failed attempt | none: the hook drops it | required: `0` in `bound`; in `bind-failed`, at most 3 digits | case 11 where missing, not digits, over 3 digits, or not 0 in `bound` (code:20440–20442). A `bind-failed` record with `rc=0` is a `TERM` the outer trapped after its body's own 0: "stopped by a signal" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 11; code:22603) |
| `why` | one line | kept from a failed attempt | none | in `bind-failed` | case 6 says "the record gives no reason" in its place (code:22060) |
| `note=` | one line each, repeated | none | none | in `bound` only | none owed is said only by the body's facts file (the `note=` row) |

- **A new attempt** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 5):
  - The hook's rewrite at the next `2` keeps the identity, `utc`, `act`, the five copies and `ended_utc`, and sets `bind=k+1`, which counts attempts only (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233).
  - It drops `rc`, `why`, the `note=` lines, `pid`, `pidstart`, `out` and the attempt before's timing keys, and writes the new attempt's keys, as at every `2` (above). The new `out` names a file at the next files' index (section 1; lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233).
  - **A pending record that still holds the id, first** (Y:5511; tasks:1430 at `f0c7d096`; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 5): T510's child asks whether a `start-record` pending record of this transcript, on this host and container, still holds the record's copied `write_id`. Where the record holds no `write_id`, or none still holds it, there is no `w₀` wait and no lookup: the body binds anew, its `start-record` a plain run whose id T412 gives (code:8532–8553; lane 4's corrections, #165 6880140531, C1 and C2), and the copies go at the outcome, which drops them as every outcome does, and at an outcome other than 0 takes them afresh (below; code:22764–22766).
  - Only where one still holds it does T510's child read the kept `ended_utc` and copies for its `w₀` wait and its lookup (tasks:1224–1225; Y:5511).
  - The body makes the lookup, inside the bound (Y:5511; `F₀` in Y's sum; lane 3's ruling, #165 6841918192, point 10). At each outcome other than 0 the outer writes the five copies from `start-record`'s pending record as it then stands, and none where there is none or it holds no `call` line (Y:5431; tasks:1427 at `b8e3df74`). So they go where the lookup's outcome removed that record (absent, which binds anew under a new id, and LR007 `another-write`; Y:5516, Y:5518), each only where `guard_bind_settle` succeeds, and stay where it kept it (a 3, Y:5517, and LR007 `generation`, Y:5519).
  - **Where the settle fails,** the pending record and the copies stay (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S3). After `absent` the body then ends 3 and keeps the pending record (12eddddf:22962–22968). After LR007 `another-write` it still ends 2, the pending record stays, and the outer gathers the copies again from it at the outcome (12eddddf:22976–22978, :22756–22760). Its `why=` then says that the pending record could not be settled, so it and its copies are kept, and that the next `2` looks it up again, as T502's part 14 corrects it (66a0f1e3:22989–22995); at `12eddddf` it said the pending record is removed (12eddddf:22977). **What a failed settle leaves** in `register-pending/` (66a0f1e3:22833–22841): the pending record, untouched; the attempt directories its `call=` lines name, which the settle removes only after the record (66a0f1e3:22838–22839); and, where the settle took its claim and then could not remove the record, a higher claim having appeared or the record's `rm` having failed, its own claim, `<record>.take.<k>`, which it does not release (66a0f1e3:22837–22838, :8320–8322), with the higher claim, where there is one. That claim names the body's pid, `$$` in the settle's subshell being the body's, and the body's start token where `pid_start_of` read it, else none (66a0f1e3:7963–7965). Once the body has ended, the claim, read on its own host and container, reads dead where its pid names no process, or only a zombie (66a0f1e3:7888; `pid_start_of` answers 1 for both, 66a0f1e3:13673, :13693, :13704), and where the pid is another process's and the claim's token differs (66a0f1e3:7884); and a claim that names the reading run's own pid, which that run did not make, is a dead run's (66a0f1e3:7876) (lane 4's check, #165 6885294533, N1 to N3). Read on another host or container, or where either is not known, it is live for the seam's cap only (66a0f1e3:7871–7875). It follows T412's conservative rules otherwise (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6882105713, S3): with no token and the pid in use again, it is live until the seam's cap, 120 s at D7's 30 s (66a0f1e3:7880–7882, :7817–7836); and where the pid's start cannot be read, it is live with no bound, as R49 reads `pid_start_of`'s 2 (66a0f1e3:7889), and so it is on any other answer (66a0f1e3:7891–7892). Once it reads dead, the next run that settles or removes that record takes a claim above it and removes it with the record (66a0f1e3:7982–7990, :8323–8330). Where the 30 s wait passes while the highest claim's owner lives, the settle takes and leaves no claim of its own, and that owner's claim remains (66a0f1e3:7969–7977). Where `register-pending/` or its claim cannot be made, it leaves no claim of its own either (66a0f1e3:7958, :7993).
- **Each rewrite, key by key** (lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 4). A rewrite writes the keys it names, each in place of every line of that key, and drops the keys it names. It keeps every other line as it stands: the identity, `utc`, `act`, `c`, `n_a`, `w_s_ms`, keys no reader knows, and `note=` lines it does not drop (section 1; code:20460–20486). So each row names what it writes and what it drops, and the rest is kept:

| From, to | Writer | Writes | Drops | Code |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| none, to `name-drift`: the offer | the guard | the file whole: `mode`, `from`, `to`, `session`, `uuid`, `utc`, `window`, `pane`, and `act` where the offer's read was SQL | — | pin:10962–10971; code:21674–21685 |
| `name-drift`, unchanged: a `2`, before its rewrite | the hook (T507) | `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms`, each where the prefetch's `.head` gives a whole number | — | code:20561–20570 |
| `name-drift` to `binding`: a `2` | the hook | `mode`; `bind`, one above the record's, a record with no `bind` counting 0, so the first `2` writes `bind=1`; `started_utc`, `bound_s`, `bounded` and `deadline_epoch`; `host` and `container`; `out`, at the next files' index; `detach` | `rc`, `why`, the `note=` lines, `pid` and `pidstart` | code:22203–22204, :22328–22329, :22353–22356 |
| `binding` back to `name-drift`: the `2`'s child not started | the hook | `mode`; `bind`, back to the attempts spent before | `bind` where none was spent before; `started_utc`, `bound_s`, `bounded`, `deadline_epoch`, `host`, `container`, `out` and `detach` | code:22379–22382 |
| `binding`, unchanged: the outer's start | the outer | `started_utc`, `bound_s`, `bounded` and `deadline_epoch`; `pid`; `pidstart` where `pid_start_of $$` exited 0 | `pidstart` where it exited 2 | code:22529–22530, :22543–22544 |
| `binding` to `bound`, only over its own `binding`, which it reads again after it has gathered what the outcome writes (section 1; lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S6) | the outer | `mode`, `rc=0`, the `note=` lines and `ended_utc` | `why` and the five copies | 12eddddf:22756–22760, then :22790–22794, the order since a326c043:22731–22735, then :22758–22760 (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S6) |
| `binding` to `bind-failed`, only over its own `binding`, which it reads again after it has gathered the five copies (section 1; lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S6) | the outer | `mode`, `rc`, `why` and `ended_utc`; the five copies where `start-record`'s pending record holds a `call` line | the `note=` lines; the five copies where none is taken | 12eddddf:22756–22760, then :22790–22794, the order since a326c043:22731–22735, then :22758–22760 (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S6) |
| `bound` to removed: case 5 | the guard | — | the record and `<uuid>.bind.*` | code:20359–20362, :22043–22044 |
| `bind-failed` to `name-drift`: cases 6 and 7 | the guard | `mode` | nothing: `bind=k` stays, and so do the failed attempt's `rc`, `why`, `ended_utc`, copies and attempt keys, which the next `2` drops or keeps as its row gives | code:22058 |
| `binding` to `name-drift`: case 9 | the guard | `mode`, `rc=unknown` and `ended_utc`, the guard's time; in phase 2 the five copies, as at an outcome | the five copies where none is taken | code:22145–22146 |
| `binding` to `name-drift`: case 10, after `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s | the guard | `mode`, `rc=unknown` and `ended_utc` | the five copies | code:22088 |
| a bind mode to removed: case 11 | the guard | — | the record alone; `<uuid>.bind.*` stays for the next removal | code:22031–22032 |
| `name-drift` to `allow`: choice 1 | the guard | the file whole: `mode=allow`, `from`, `to`, `session`, `uuid`, and `utc`, the answer's time | every other key, and `<uuid>.bind.*` | code:22246–22259 |
| `name-drift` to removed: choice 3 typed, or an offer whose identity no longer holds | the guard | — | the record and `<uuid>.bind.*` | code:22235–22236, :22399 |
| `name-drift` to removed: an offer `guard_answer` cannot read | the guard | — | the record alone | code:22218–22219 |

## 4. The bound

- **One helper** computes `bound_s` from the record, for the hook's "BINDING" text and for the outer's start, "so the two never differ" (tasks:604, :616). The hook runs it for its `mode=binding` rewrite, and the outer at its start (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4).
- **The sum** (Y:5459): `B`, plus one read's `T_r` + `w_s` to spare, plus the 10 s of `-k`, rounded up to 10 s. "That is 437 + 28`N_a`·`c` + 19`w_s` s, rounded up to 10 s: 500 s at `c` = 2 and 530 s at `c` = 3, with or without the service", but "the bound is 540 s only for a p90 between about 274 and 276 ms", and 590 s at Eagle's p90, where `c` = 5 and `w_s` = 0.656 s (Y:5459). With two addresses it is 550 s and 610 s, or 560 s and 620 s with the service (Y:5462).
- **In bash 3.2's integers,** from the record's keys (tasks:616; B2:140): `bound_s = (437000 + 28000·n_a·c + 19·w_s_ms + 9999) / 10000 · 10`.
- **The 540 s band, in those integers:** with one address and `c` = 3, `w_s_ms` = 473 gives (437000 + 84000 + 8987 + 9999) / 10000 · 10 = 530, and `w_s_ms` = 474 gives (437000 + 84000 + 9006 + 9999) / 10000 · 10 = 540. The helper computes the sum, never a table of Y's round figures (Y:5459; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 7).
- **The defaults,** where the keys are absent: `c` = 2, `n_a` = 1, `w_s_ms` = 0, which give 500 s (tasks:616; CLI44:304). They apply to every bind before T507 lands, and to every record that lacks the keys (tasks:616, :747).
- **Malformed inputs:** a `c`, `n_a` or `w_s_ms` that is not a whole number, a `c` below 2, or an `n_a` below 1 counts as absent, so its default applies (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 10; Y:3810–3812, where a `connect_timeout` under 2 is refused). So does one too long for the shell's integers: a `c` or `n_a` over 6 digits, or a `w_s_ms` over 9, its leading zeros dropped first (code:20500–20506, :20543–20548).
- **`LANES_BIND_BOUND_S`** overrides the sum, for tests (Y:5429; tasks:616; Ytp:6201). AT-F6 sets `LANES_BIND_BOUND_S=15`, "under which the body runs under `timeout -k 10 5` and `deadline_epoch` is the start plus 20 s" (Ytp:6020–6021).
  - It is honoured only as a whole number of at least 11. Any other value is ignored, and the sum is kept (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 10, after tasks:1423's rule for the service's intervals).
  - At 10 the body's `timeout -k 10 0` runs with no limit, and below 10 `timeout` refuses with 125 (coreutils 9.4: both checked by draft 1's writer, and the first by lane 3's analysis).
- **`deadline_epoch`** is the start plus `bound_s` plus 5 s (Y:5429): at the default, 505 s after `started_utc` (Ytp:6044–6045).
- **The body's `timeout`** is `timeout -k 10 "$(( bound_s - 10 ))"` (Y:5397): 490 s at the default (Ytp:5985).
- **`bounded=yes`** where `timeout` exists. **`bounded=no`** where it is missing, as on macOS without coreutils: the body runs unbounded, started under `set -m` so that it leads a group of its own, and the outer's trap sends `kill -TERM -- -<body pid>`, then `KILL` 10 s later if the group is still there (Y:5402–5405; tasks:611).
- **The outer's sweep** (R47; ERR-10): after `timeout` returns 124 or 137, or after the outer's trap fired, the outer sends `TERM` to the group `timeout` led, then `KILL` 10 s later if any member is left, and only then writes the outcome. It never sweeps after the body's own exit, 0 or not (tasks:610; L5:527–528). The sweep ends by `bound_s` at the latest, inside `deadline_epoch` (tasks:610).
  - The sweep keys on `timeout`'s 124 or 137 and on the trap, as tasks:610 gives it, whatever the time. Point 16(b)'s test of the time sorts only the `why` (section 3; lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 16(b)). A 137 inside the bound is the body's own end only for the `why`: its group is still swept, so nothing it started is left running (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, reading 2).
- **What the bound is:** "`bound_s` of wall time from `started_utc`, over the child's whole body" (Y:5436). It "never stops a child whose every step stays inside its own bound" (Y:5436). In files mode it "is a judgment" (Y:5483).

## 5. The guard's eleven cases, in short

The check is a branch of its own at the offer site, after 18(h)'s count and before the table, on `mode` in `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed` (Y:5493; tasks:588). It costs one small file read, `kill -0` and one `/proc` read, about 7 ms (Y:5493). Every text goes to stderr, and the hook string is unchanged (Y:5493; FR-040). The texts are Y's, in short (Y:5497–5507).

| # | Case | Exit | Record after | Text, in short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | the answer `2` itself | 2 | `mode=binding` | "BINDING: this window is now named `<to>` … detached (pid `<p>`, bounded at `<bound_s>` s) … send your work again in a minute or so; until it ends, each prompt is answered BINDING IN PROGRESS" |
| 2 | same session; child live, inside the bound | 2 | unchanged | "BINDING IN PROGRESS: … it ends by `<deadline utc>` at the latest. Your prompt was NOT sent; send it again in a few seconds". Where the record says `bounded=no`, the text leaves out "at the latest" |
| 3 | same session; child live past `deadline_epoch` | 2 | unchanged | "THE BIND HAS RUN PAST ITS `<bound_s>` s BOUND and is still running (pid `<p>`, started `<utc>`)". With `bounded=no`: "this host has no `timeout`, so nothing stops it; it is most likely in a `git` call". With `bounded=yes`: "its `timeout` has not ended it; it is likely stuck on the disk or the network". Then what waiting costs and what `kill <p>` costs, and "Your prompt was NOT sent." Where the outer is dead and processes of its session are still running, case 3 has its own text: "…its outer (pid `<p>`) has died and processes of its session are still running … `pkill -s <p>` stops them … no outcome will be written" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 9) |
| 4 | another session wanting the same lane | its own table | its own record | it never sees this record. If it answers `2`, on primary `start-record`'s `bind:<to>` expectation refuses whichever bind commits second, with 2; in files mode there is no second `binding` read: `lane-start`'s re-read at `:2614` runs only after a handoff wait or a Claude resolution (`bind_recheck`, `:2611`), and `--no-launch --bind` on a free lane has neither, so two binds that both read the lane free both land (design:5547 at `f0c7d096`; AT-F6 row (d)) |
| 5 | after success, `mode=bound` | 2, once | removed where it is still the `bound` loaded; otherwise nothing is removed, and the record is answered once (section 1) | "MOVED: this window is lane `<to>`, bound to this session (`<uuid>`) at `<ended_utc>`." Here `<uuid>` is this session's, and where `ended_utc` is missing the line says "a time the record does not give" in its place. Then one line per owed note, "  still owed: `<note>`", and "This prompt was NOT sent: it is the one that hears how the bind ended. Send your work again." The next prompt exits 0 |
| 6 | after failure, `mode=bind-failed`, attempt `k` below 3 | 2, once | `mode=name-drift`, `bind=k`; the window renamed back to `<from>` | "THE BIND TO LANE `<to>` FAILED (exit `<rc>`): `<why>` — its output was written to `<out>` … the offer is KEPT: reply `1`, `2` or `3`", the clause on its output where the record names an `out`, and "was written to" since T502's part 53, in place of "is kept in", since another prompt's choice 1 can sweep that `.out` meanwhile (lane 2's independent review of part 51, NIT-5; 00e2c92e:22871–22874) |
| 7 | failure on the third attempt | 2 | `mode=name-drift`, `bind=3`; the window renamed back to `<from>` | as case 6, and choice 2 is no longer offered |
| 8 | does a failure restart it? | — | — | never by itself: a failed bind spends the person's yes; each `2` is one attempt, at most 3 per offer |
| 9 | stale: `mode=binding`, same host and container; its pid reused, or dead with no process left in the session it led | 2, once | `mode=name-drift`, `rc=unknown`, `ended_utc` the guard's time, and in phase 2 the five copies; the window renamed back to `<from>` | "THE BIND STARTED AT `<utc>` … IS NO LONGER RUNNING AND LEFT NO OUTCOME … the next `2` finds out first" |
| 10 | `mode=binding` from another host or container | 2 | unchanged until `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s, then as case 9 but with no copies; case 10 renames nothing, save in a window this answer renamed, where since T502's part 62, past `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s and after its rewrite back, it renames back as case 9 does, save where a failed restore's read again finds a bind record that is not the one loaded; before that time it keeps the window, and on its rewrite back's 2 `guard_site_moved` answers it as for cases 6 and 9 | "A BIND OF THIS TRANSCRIPT IS RUNNING ON `<host>/<container>` … Your prompt was NOT sent". Where the record says `bounded=no`, the text adds that the child there may still be running, and that on primary `bind:<to>` refuses whichever bind commits second. Past `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s, its note ends "This window is not renamed: that bind ran on another host or container."; at T502's part 61, in a window this answer renamed, it ended "This window keeps the name `<to>` that this answer gave it; that bind ran on another host or container, so case 10 renames nothing.", at the cost, which part 61's comment stated, that the offer kept there was about `<from>`, so the next prompt, in a window named for `<to>`, dropped it as stale and judged the prompt afresh (lane 1's check of parts 58 and 59, NIT-3; 475a04a4:23146–23156; lane 3's review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e`, NIT-1). Since T502's part 62, `6d2bc47a`, as lane 3 rules (#165 6898801339; lane 3's review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e`, SHOULD-3), where this answer renamed nothing the note ends as before; and in a window this answer renamed, case 10 renames the window back to this answer's `<from>` (`GBT_REN_FROM`), as case 9 does on an unknown outcome on this host (ERR-23), though case 9 renames back to the record's own `from`: the note ends "That bind ran on another host or container, but this answer renamed this window here; the next note says what came of renaming it back.", and `guard_site_window_back` follows it. After a restore that worked, the rename back is in the default form, and "The offer is KEPT:" follows, with the choices, where the record loaded is from this answer's `<from>` (since part 63; below). After a restore that failed, the record is read again (`guard_site_reread`), and the rename back takes `guard_site_back_form`'s form for what that read found: the default, for the record loaded or an offer between the same two lanes; `changed`; `unread`; or `bind`, which leaves the window as it is and NOT renamed back, for a bind record that is not the one loaded, so that, of these outcomes, on that one path case 10 keeps a window this answer renamed, as cases 6 and 9 do. `guard_site_unrestored`'s note then follows, and the choices only where an offer of this window's is kept, as for case 9 (the bullet on cases 6, 7, 9 and 10, below). Where the rename back fails, `guard_window_back`'s note says so, as for case 9, in the default form: "This window could NOT be renamed back to `<from>`, so it still carries `<to>`'s name, and the next prompt would drop the kept offer as one about another window. Rename it first: tmux rename-window -t `<this window>` `<from>`", so the choices printed after it hold once the window is renamed by hand. That bind ran on another host or container and is past its deadline, so renaming back meets no veto 2 of its own, and part 61's words, and their cost, are gone. **The window left** is case 9's, as part 62's comment states it: a bind another prompt starts here between the restore, or the read again, and the rename back has its rename undone, and its `lane-start` refuses it (veto 2); and case 9's displaced-record windows (section 1) (eb509d24:22485–22489, :22536, :23153–23183; lane 3's delta review of parts 10 and 11, BLOCKING-1, NIT-1 and NIT-4, spec#65 6899733577). Since T502's part 63, `fcc635ed`, where the record case 10 loaded is from another `from` than this answer's (`GBR_FROM` is not `GBT_REN_FROM`), the rename back still undoes this answer's own rename, but in `changed`'s words where the default form would be printed; after a restore that worked, "The offer restored there is about lane `<GBR_FROM>`, not this window's lane `<GBT_REN_FROM>`, so no choices are printed: the next prompt reads whatever is there." is printed in place of "The offer is KEPT:"; and no choices are printed, after a restore that worked or one that failed, since no offer of this window's is kept there (lane 3's delta review of part 62, NIT-2, #165 6899381832; 1604ef38:23189–23209; case 9's rename back, :23273). The outcomes above are those past `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s, after the rewrite back. Before that time, while that bind may still run, case 10 answers as in progress, renames nothing back, and so keeps a window this answer renamed, and `guard_answer_moved`'s last note says so, "This window keeps the name `<to>` that this answer gave it."; and where its rewrite back returns 2, `guard_site_moved` answers as for cases 6 and 9 (the bullet on cases 6, 7, 9 and 10, below): it renames that window back itself only for an offer or an allowance from this answer's `<from>`, and a bind record it finds there goes to `guard_bind_site` once more (lane 3's delta review of part 12, SHOULD-A, spec#65 6900603360; 1604ef38:23149–23156, :23163–23164, :22811–22850, :23372–23374) |
| 11 | an unreadable record: one missing or malforming any of `mode`, `from`, `to`, `bind` (1 to 3, the attempt, which the files' index leaves unchanged: lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233), `host`, `container`, `deadline_epoch` (digits), or `rc` in `bound` or `bind-failed` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4). T502's code bounds two of them, so that no value overflows the shell's integers: `deadline_epoch` at most 12 digits, and `rc` at most 3, which is 0 in `bound` (code:20437–20442; section 3's grammar). A malformed `pid` or `pidstart` does not join this set (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15) | 2 | dropped; `<uuid>.bind.*` left for the next removal to sweep (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, points 6 and 13) | today's text: "the pending offer at `<f>` could not be read, so it has been dropped rather than answered on a guess. Send your prompt again and the question will be asked afresh." (pin:11091–11094, which Y:5507 cites as `:11097–11101`: ERR-24; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 14). Since T502's part 55, a record is case 11's only where its last read finds it unreadable: a bind mode that `guard_answer` reads is `guard_bind_site`'s, an allowance is its 3, and an offer or an allowance that `guard_bind_site`'s load reads is refused as changed, "the pending offer at `<f>` was changed while it was being answered (it says mode=`<m>` now), so nothing was written to it. Send your prompt again and it will be read afresh.", and kept. Since part 58, a record gone by the drop's existence test is `guard_bind_gone`'s, "the pending offer at `<f>` was gone when it came to be answered, so nothing was written to it. Send your prompt again and it will be read afresh.", and nothing is dropped. Since part 56, a drop whose `rm` fails is `guard_drop_refuse`'s, never "dropped" (section 1; de96ef86:22912–22972, :23399–23437) |

- **"Child live"** in cases 2 and 3 is section 6's test, including a dead outer whose session still has a process (Y:5505; svc:160–162). That session is tested only where the record says `detach=setsid` or `detach=python3` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 8).
- **A `binding` record without a well-formed `pid` and `pidstart`** (with no `pid`, before the outer's first rewrite or where the outer never ran; with `pid` alone, where the outer's `pid_start_of $$` exited 2; or with either malformed) gets case 2's "BINDING IN PROGRESS", with no pid, until `deadline_epoch`. After that it is case 9, with no session test (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4; lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15; Y:5505; R49). A record with `pid` alone, and `pidstart` empty, is one of these: in lane 3's words, "That is 6747722621's 'without a well-formed `pid` and `pidstart`'" (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, reading 1).
  - **Well-formed:** `pid` is decimal digits, with no leading 0, above 1, and at most 10 digits (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 17). `pidstart` is not empty, and on Linux is all digits, the one token `pid_start_of` prints there (section 6). The guard applies this rule before it calls anything: "above 1" is the record's own rule, not `pid_start_of`'s (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15, as lane 3's ruling, #165 6747722621 narrows it).
  - **The 10-digit cap** is `pid_start_of`'s own (section 6). An 11-digit `pid` is malformed, so it reads as no `pid`: "BINDING IN PROGRESS" until `deadline_epoch`, then case 9. Lane 3's why: without the cap, such a record holds case 3 for ever, since R49 reads `pid_start_of`'s 2 as live; with it, the record ends at the deadline, as every other malformed `pid` does. No real pid comes near 10 digits (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 17).
  - **A malformed value is never passed** to `kill`, `pid_start_of`, `pgrep -s` or a text (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15).
  - **Why it waits, and is not dropped by case 11:** dropping the record during a live bind is the wrong "dead" that R49 forbids. Waiting is Y's own fallback: "the row waits for `deadline_epoch` instead" (Y:5505). Case 11 already requires a well-formed `deadline_epoch`, so the wait ends.
  - **Why a bad `pid` is dangerous, not only stuck:** with `pid=0`, `pgrep -s 0` matches the caller's own session, and `kill -0 0` tests the caller's own process group, so the bind would read as live for ever. Case 3's dead-outer text would tell the person to run `pkill -s 0`, which kills their own session (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15).
- **Case 10's 60 s** "covers a `git` call that outlives the body under its own `timeout` (50 s, P1), plus 10 s between the two hosts' clocks". Where `bounded=yes`, the old write's `not_after` has passed by then (Y:5506).
- **Case 6's rename back** fixes today's finding: the window kept `<to>`'s name, so the kept offer was dropped (Y:5509; tasks:625).
- **Cases 7 and 9 rename the window back to `<from>`,** as case 6 does, and case 10 renames nothing (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 7; ERR-23, research:359–367; svc:124, svc:156–158; Ytp:6065–6068; 007-spec:490–493). T502's lines for the two, tasks:596 and :598, follow in B4. Since T502's part 62, case 10 renames back too, but only in a window this answer renamed, and with the exceptions case 10's row gives, as lane 3 rules (#165 6898801339; case 10's row, above).
- **Case 1's `<bound_s>`** comes from section 4's helper, and the hook makes no register call of its own for answer 2 (tasks:604).
- **Where the record is gone or changed under a rewrite,** each caller's result, by lane 3's ruling 5, "No rewrite creates a record", which holds outside each rewrite's window from its read to its `mv` (section 1), and lane 5's rule for the hook's writes on the offer it answered (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, ruling 5; lane 3's confirmations, #165 6886113842; section 1), as T502's parts 22 to 41 build them at `6253b0ed`:
  - **Case 1, where the offer is gone, no longer says `name-drift`, or is no longer the offer that was answered, at the hook's head copy or its rewrite to `binding`:** nothing is written, and the record's mode is read once (6253b0ed:22908–22913, :22935–22943, :22641–22700).
    - **Gone, or any mode but those below: (A).** The guard prints the three names, tries the rename back where this answer renamed the window, and words its refusal on what came of it: "`<why>`, so the bind was NOT started: `<window>`, NOTHING has been written to the register or to `<file>`, and no outer was started. Send your prompt again and the question will be asked afresh." (part 31; :22682–22695). `<why>` is "the offer was dropped before the bind could be recorded" where it is gone; "the offer at `<file>` was changed before the bind could be recorded (it is another offer now, not the one that was answered)" where a `name-drift` offer is there; and "the offer at `<file>` was changed before the bind could be recorded (it says mode=`<m>` now)" for any other mode (:22675–22681). `<window>` is "this window has NOT moved to `<to>`" where this answer renamed nothing; "this window has NOT moved to `<to>` (this answer renamed it, and it was renamed back to `<from>`)"; or "this window was renamed to `<to>` by this answer and could NOT be renamed back to `<from>` (see the note above)" (:22687–22694). Since T502's part 50, a record that is there and could not be read again, a file it cannot read or a path that is not a regular file, is no longer said to be "mode=none" or "dropped": `<why>` is then "the offer at `<file>` was changed before the bind could be recorded, and the record there could not be read again", and the refusal ends "Send your prompt again: the next prompt reads whatever is there." in place of "…and the question will be asked afresh." (part 50's audit of part 49's NIT-3; 00e2c92e:23061–23087, :23102). It prints no BINDING, starts no outer, and does not say that the offer is KEPT; since part 45 the rename back's own note takes `guard_window_back`'s `none` form, which says the answer `2` started no bind, and not that an offer is kept, as do the I/O paths below where the answered offer is no longer there (00e2c92e:22390–22435, :23096). Since T502's part 57, where the head copy had written the prefetch's keys into the offer before the rewrite to `binding` found it changed, "NOTHING has been written to the register or to `<file>`" is in its place "NOTHING has been written to the register, and this answer's one write at `<file>` was its copy of the prefetch's `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` into the offer" (lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, NIT 3; de96ef86:23283–23291). This answer writes nothing to the record, and at `00e2c92e` renamed nothing back (since part 58, below), and the site answers it by this section's cases: a `binding` is left byte for byte and answered as in progress, a `bound` is heard as the first read hears it and dropped where it is still the one loaded, and a `bind-failed` is answered as failed and rewritten to the question. Where the site's load, or `guard_site_moved`'s read, then finds an offer or an allowance whose `from`, read with its mode, is this answer's `<from>`, no bind holds the window and the answer started none, so it is renamed back in the `none` form before the refusal (:22737–22748, :22774–22777, :22937–22948). Every other refusal keeps the window named for `<to>`, since the bind record this prompt loaded may have ended `bound` and been heard by another prompt, case 5's MOVED, so that the lane is `<to>` now: a record gone, unreadable, a bind record, an offer or an allowance from another lane, or any other mode (:22774–22779, :22943–22948 and :22950–22961; lane 3's delta review of follow-up 3's part 1, NIT-1, #165 6915955296). **The window left, and its cost,** as part 59's comments state it: where that bind did not succeed, the window keeps `<to>`'s name though no bind of this answer's ran, and the next prompt is judged in a window named for `<to>` while the register still holds this transcript under `<from>`, so it is refused or asked on that state until the window is renamed by hand (:22744–22748, :22957–22961). Since T502's part 61, `475a04a4`, every path that keeps a window this answer renamed says so: `guard_answer_moved`'s last note is "This window keeps the name `<to>` that this answer gave it.", unless `guard_drop_refuse` or case 10 has already said it (`GBT_REN_SAID`), or `guard_window_back` was called (`GWB_CALLED`), whose own note says what came of the rename back; both flags are 0 at file scope (lane 1's check of parts 58 and 59, NIT-3; 475a04a4:20530, :22365, :22508–22511, :23314–23325). (A)'s own rename back, and its allowance branch's, stay as they are, for a gone record, for any offer or allowance, and for any other mode: part 59's rule rests on a bind record read, which shows that another answer `2` ran and may have succeeded, and there the record read just after this answer's own rewrite was no bind record, so nothing shows that any bind ran (NIT-2). **The window left, and its cost,** as part 61's comment states it: where, inside a stalled hook, a bind ran there, ended `bound` and was heard and dropped, renaming back undoes its rename, and the next prompt meets a window named for `<from>` in a lane now `<to>`, refused or asked on that state until it is renamed by hand (:22782–22795). Before that, while that bind may still run, case 10 answers as in progress and keeps the window, and `guard_answer_moved`'s last note says so; and on its rewrite's 2 `guard_site_moved` answers as for cases 6 and 9, and that note follows only where the window is kept, since a rename back sets `GWB_CALLED` (lane 3's delta review of part 12, SHOULD-A, spec#65 6900603360; lane 3's delta review of follow-up 2's part 1, NIT-4, spec#70 6901055715; 1604ef38:23149–23156, :23163–23164, :22811–22850, :23372–23374, :22526). Case 10 no longer sets `GBT_REN_SAID` itself: where it renames back, its call of `guard_window_back` sets `GWB_CALLED`; where the `bind` form leaves the window, that form sets `GBT_REN_SAID`, where `GBT_REN_FROM` is set, so that no second "keeps the name" note follows case 6's, case 9's or case 10's "left as it is" (lane 1's check of parts 60 and 61, NIT (a), #165 6898019939; lane 3's delta review of parts 10 and 11, BLOCKING-1, spec#65 6899733577); and `guard_answer_moved` takes its first `mode` line in `guard_site_reread`'s form too, the newline in a variable, so that no `$'…'` sits inside a quoted `${…}` (lane 3's review of `00e2c92e..475a04a4`, NIT-3; eb509d24:22933–22942, :23324–23328). Otherwise, since part 49, the words name what this answer wrote there (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, NIT-2): `<did>` is "this answer's one write at `<file>` was its copy of the prefetch's `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` into the offer" where the head copy wrote, as `GHC_WROTE` records, set to 1 only where the head copy's rewrite returned 0, and otherwise "this answer changed nothing at `<file>`" (:20829–20846, :23123–23132). Where the record is there and could not be read again (NIT-3), the refusal is "the bind could not be recorded in `<file>`, so it was NOT started: NOTHING has been written to the register, and `<did>`, and the record there could not be read again. Send your prompt again: the next prompt reads whatever is there." (:23133–23134). Otherwise, where the head copy wrote, it is "the bind could not be recorded in `<file>`, so it was NOT started: NOTHING has been written to the register, and `<did>`; the record there is no longer the offer that was answered (`<now>`). Send your prompt again: the next prompt reads whatever is there." (:23135–23136); and where it did not, it is part 45's, unchanged: "the bind could not be recorded in `<file>`, so it was NOT started: NOTHING has been written to the register, and this answer changed nothing at `<file>`, where the record is no longer the offer that was answered (`<now>`). Send your prompt again: the next prompt reads whatever is there." (:23137–23138). `<now>` is "it says mode=`<m>` now", "it could not be read again" or "it is gone now" (:20435–20441). In each of these three the window is renamed back in the `none` form (:23140). Each is true at the instant of its re-read only.
  - **The hook's back-out, where no child started:** it runs only on a record that still says `binding` (`--binding`), and the guard prints the three names and tries the rename back first (part 31; 00e2c92e:23496–23547). `<window>` is "this window has NOT moved to `<to>`", "this window has NOT moved to `<to>` (it was renamed back to `<from>`)", or "this window was renamed to `<to>` and could NOT be renamed back to `<from>` (see the note above)" (:23527–23535). Where the record is gone or no longer `binding`, there is nothing of this answer's to back out, nothing is written, the rename back takes the `none` form, and the refusal is "the bind's child could not be started, so `<window>` and NOTHING has been written; the offer was dropped or changed meanwhile, so there is none of this answer's to keep. Send your prompt again and the question will be asked afresh." (:23516–23518, :23536–23537). Where the rewrite fails as I/O, the record is read again, since part 45 (:23516–23524; S6). Where it is still this answer's `binding`, by its `bind`, `started_utc` and `out`, with no `pid`, the refusal is "the bind's child could not be started, so `<window>` and NOTHING has been started; but the record could not be written back to the question, so it still says binding: the next prompts say BINDING IN PROGRESS until its deadline, and the first after it finds the record stale and asks afresh. Where the record is there and could not be read again, since part 49 (NIT-3), the rename back takes the `none` form, and the refusal is "the bind's child could not be started, so `<window>` and NOTHING has been started; the record could not be written back to the question, and it could not be read again: this answer changed nothing more there, and the next prompt reads whatever is there. Send your prompt again." (:23520–23522, :23540–23541). Otherwise the rename back takes the `none` form, and the refusal is "the bind's child could not be started, so `<window>` and NOTHING has been started; the record could not be written back to the question, and it is no longer this answer's binding (`<now>`): this answer changed nothing more there, and the next prompt reads whatever is there. Send your prompt again." (:23542–23543). Each is true at the instant of its re-read only. Since T502's part 60, `f2e545b8`, the refusal where the record is gone or no longer this answer's `binding` ends "Send your prompt again and it will be read afresh." in place of "…and the question will be asked afresh.", since the record there can be another attempt's live `binding`, which the next prompt answers BINDING IN PROGRESS and never asks (lane 5's adversarial read of parts 56 to 59, NIT-1; f2e545b8:23817–23822). The note is then "This window is left as it is and NOT renamed back to `<from>`: the record now holds another attempt's bind record (it says mode=`<m>` now), `<next>`.", `<next>` being, for a `binding`, "so the window is left as that bind leaves it"; for a `bound`, "so the window is left as that bind left it, and the next prompt hears how it ended" (case 5's MOVED); and for a `bind-failed`, "so the window is left to that record's own answer: the next prompt meets it, and its failed bind's answer renames the window back" (case 6 or 7, ERR-23). `<window>` is then "this window keeps the name `<to>` that this answer gave it", and the refusal is otherwise as above. Anything else is renamed back as before. So the rename back under another attempt's live `binding`, whose cost was that bind's veto 2, is no longer made (lane 3's review of `00e2c92e..475a04a4`, NIT-1, #165 6898801339; lane 3's review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e`, NIT-2; eb509d24:23859–23879, :23884–23897, :23905–23916). Since T502's part 63, `fcc635ed` and `1604ef38`, only a read made in that block counts: `GRS_THERE` is 0 before the back-out's `case`, so on its 0, where no read is made, the window is renamed back as before, and is never kept on what an earlier `guard_record_says` in the same hook run left, `guard_drop_if`'s in a first `guard_answer`'s stale drop or in `guard_allow_drop` (lane 3's delta review of part 62, SHOULD-1, #165 6899381832); and `GRS_THERE`, `GRS_MODE`, `GRS_OUT`, `GRS_GOT` and `GRS_SEEN` are reset at file scope, as `guard_record_says` resets them, so that values the environment carries, such as `GRS_THERE=1 GRS_MODE=binding`, are never read as a read this prompt made (lane 1's check of part 62, #165 6899534219; 1604ef38:22377–22382, :23896–23918). **A limit for v1.3** (lane 3's delta review of part 62, NIT-3; section 10's point 27): the keep reads the record's mode only, not its `host=` and `container=`, and its reason, that renaming back would undo that bind's rename so that its `lane-start` refuses it (veto 2), holds only for a bind on this host; for another host's or container's bind record the window is kept, named for `<to>`, all the same, and the next prompt is judged in a window named for `<to>`. A later part may test `host=` and `container=`.

A record that is there and cannot be read is the I/O failure, "the explicit allow could not be recorded at `<file>`, so the mismatch remains unapproved. Nothing was renamed or written; answer 1 again after the session state is writable." (part 34; :22805–22808, :22820). Where the `mv` is made, the `.bind.*` names listed before the check are swept only where they are the offer's own and their index is canonical, `[1-9][0-9]*`: at or below the index of the `out` the answered offer names at its one read, or, where it names none, every canonical name so listed. A listed name whose index is not canonical is skipped, and survives choice 1, where at `6253b0ed` choice 1 removed every `.bind.*` name (parts 44 and 47; 00e2c92e:23290, :23319–23337, the test at :23328 and :23335; 6253b0ed:22823; lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, NIT-2). The names are then listed again, and one not listed before, above the offer's own, is a concurrent answer `2`'s attempt: in place of ALLOWED the person is told "THIS ANSWER `1` MET AN ANSWER `2` MADE AT THE SAME MOMENT: that answer may have started a bind of this window to lane `<to>` (its output is `<out>`), and this allowance was written over its record at `<file>`. The allowance is recorded. If that bind started, it is NOT stopped: its outer leaves a record that is no longer its own, so how the bind ends is written only to that output, which is kept; if its outer had not yet made its start rewrite, it runs nothing, and says so there. Read that output to see which, and whether this window was bound to lane `<to>`; the next prompt reads the record and the window as they are then. This answer is consumed; send your work again." (part 44; 00e2c92e:23338–23357). Where the outer's start rewrite read `binding` before choice 1's `mv` and makes its own `mv` after it, that rewrite's window writes the outer's `binding` over the allowance, and the bind runs with its record its own, so three of the note's clauses are false: "The allowance is recorded"; "its outer leaves a record that is no longer its own, so how the bind ends is written only to that output", since its outcome reaches the record (00e2c92e:23740–23749, :23924–23927); and "if its outer had not yet made its start rewrite, it runs nothing" (section 1; lane 1's R1, #165 6894040564, NIT-1, which lane 3's ruling on point 27 does not name, confirmed under it by lane 3, #165 6894714082, a reading lane 3 confirms in its review of spec#65 at `41b70a8e`, spec#65 6898800291, call (a); lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, NIT 2). "May have started" is part 49's: the second listing also sees a `.out` whose outer has not yet made its start rewrite, and that outer then finds the allowance, runs nothing, and says so in that output (lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47, NIT-6; :23351–23354). Its window is in section 1.
  - **The outer's start, since T502's part 21:** where the record is gone, or no longer says `binding`, when the outer rewrites it at its start, the outer writes nothing, runs nothing and returns 2 (section 7). The file then holds no record, or the record that took this one's place, and the guard answers that at the next prompt: by this section's cases where it says `binding`, `bound` or `bind-failed`, and otherwise as the offer site does today (beeae571:21434–21442). Lane 2's case for it is in section 8.
- **"BINDING" and "BINDING IN PROGRESS"** (R58; ERR-14): "BINDING" is answer 2's own text, and "BINDING IN PROGRESS" each prompt's while the bind runs. Y:5389's "binding in progress" for answer 2 is history.

## 6. Liveness, and `pid_start_of`

- **The test** (Y:5434): "the pid is live and its start time equals `pidstart`". It is judged only where the record's `host` and `container` are this guard's (Y:5423). Otherwise case 10 answers.
- **`pid_start_of <pid>`** (Q-L5-6, L5:516–519; R49):
  - **Argument:** one pid.
    - It exits 2 for an argument that is not a well-formed pid in its own sense: empty, `0`, a leading `0`, a non-digit, or more than 10 digits, as T502's part 2 does at `0ebde08`. For `1` it reads `/proc/1`, and the tokens decide (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747722621, which narrows lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15).
    - It does not exit 2 for every argument the bind record's rule refuses: "above 1" is the record's own rule, which the guard applies before it calls anything (section 5). The function's contract is to read a real pid's start token, and in a container pid 1 is a real process (lane 3's ruling, #165 6747722621, upholding lane 4's view, #165 6747648353).
    - The bind record's rule takes the function's cap of 10 digits, so the guard never passes it a `pid` too long for it (section 5; lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 17).
  - **Output:** the process's start token on stdout, where it exits 0.
  - **Exit 0:** the pid is live, and its token is printed.
  - **Exit 1:** the pid is not live: it names no process, or only a zombie, which has already exited (6253b0ed:13675, :13695, :13706; lane 4's check, #165 6888910119, O1).
  - **Exit 2:** the start cannot be read, or the argument is not a well-formed pid (above). Every caller treats 2 as live, never as dead: "A wrong 'dead' would let a claim or a bind be stolen; a wrong 'live' only waits" (R49). R49 is recorded here.
  - **The token:** on Linux, field 22 of `/proc/<pid>/stat`, counted after the last `)`; on macOS, `ps -o lstart= -p <pid>`, with runs of spaces squeezed to one and its ends trimmed (L5:518; Y:5434; 6253b0ed:13716–13717; lane 4's check, #165 6888910119, O2). It is compared as a string, on the same host and container only (L5:518).
- **So the outer is live** where `pid_start_of <pid>` exits 0 and prints the record's `pidstart`, or exits 2. It is not live where it exits 1, or exits 0 with another token, which is a reused pid: "a reused pid and a dead pid both read as not live" (Y:5434).
- **One helper** "serves this record and F10's pending record" (Y:5434), whose claims' owners it tests (Y:5185; tasks:938). T502 defines it, and it lands before lane 4's T412 (L5:519).
- **Lane 4's T412 and T414** are callers that read any exit other than 0 and 1 as live (code:7874–7889, :5564–5570; #165 6747564649; lane 4's corrections, #165 6880140531, C4). That is R49's own rule, which lane 3's ruling, #165 6750702819, leaves unchanged. T412's `wr_claim_take` also calls it on its own `$$`, and reads any exit but 0 as no token (code:7961–7962; lane 4's check, #165 6888910119, O3).
- **It never copies `lock_steal_if_dead`'s pid-only test** (Y:5434; tasks:617). That test is `kill -0` on the lock's pid alone (pin:1286–1296), "under which a reused pid looks live until the 10-minute age" (Y:5434).
- **Its cost:** "P5: 200 checks take 1.42 s, about 7 ms each" (Y:5434).
- **The stale row's session test** (Y:5505; tasks:618), on a record of this host and container whose outer is not live:
  - **A reused pid** (exit 0, another token) needs no session test, and the bind is stale at once: "the kernel does not hand a pid out again while a session of that number has members" (Y:5505; Ytp:6069–6071).
  - **A dead pid** (exit 1): "`$detach` made the outer the leader of its own session", and the body, `timeout` and any `git` under its own `timeout` stay in that session after the outer dies (Y:5505). While any process has the record's `pid` as its session id, the bind is still running, the guard answers by cases 2 and 3, and no second bind starts (Y:5505; svc:160–162). Past `deadline_epoch`, case 3 then takes its dead-outer text (section 5; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 9). Once no process has, the bind is stale, case 9 (svc:156–158).
  - **The session test runs only where the record says `detach=setsid` or `detach=python3`** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 8).
  - **The probe:** `pgrep -s <pid>`, as v3f's case 6 names it, else a scan of field 6 of `/proc/*/stat` where `pgrep` is missing (tasks:618; Y:5505; Ytp:6061).
  - **Where the outer led no session** (`detach=nohup`, or no `detach` key), or the session cannot be read, the row waits for `deadline_epoch` instead (Y:5505; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 8). Lane 3's probe found a `nohup` outer in the hook's own session.
- **A `binding` record without a well-formed `pid` and `pidstart`** (section 5) has no liveness test: it reads as running until `deadline_epoch`, and then as stale with no session test. Neither value is passed to `kill`, `pid_start_of` or `pgrep -s` (section 5; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4; lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15).

## 7. Who reads the record

- **The guard,** at the offer site, at each prompt of the session (Y:5493–5507): `mode`, the identity, `host`, `container`, `pid`, `pidstart`, `bounded`, `bound_s`, `started_utc`, `deadline_epoch`, `bind`, `rc`, `why` and the `note=` lines, for section 5's cases. It also reads `detach`, for section 6's session test (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 8), and finds the attempt's own file by `out`, not by `bind` (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233). Its sweep finds the facts file from `out=` too, never from `bind` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118). It never reads `utc` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 12). `guard_answer` reads a `mode=name-drift` record as today (pin:11076–11093). In phase 2, at the stale row, it also reads `start-record`'s pending record for the five copies (Y:5431).
- **The hook's helper,** for case 1's `<bound_s>` and its `mode=binding` rewrite: `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms`, or `LANES_BIND_BOUND_S` (tasks:604, :616; lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 4).
- **Each rewrite's caller, where the record is gone, or, for the hook's writes on the offer it answered, no longer says `name-drift`** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6885771975, ruling 5; lane 3's confirmations, #165 6886113842; section 1; section 5's cases):
  - the hook's rewrite to `binding` at answer 2, `--offer` with the answered offer's `from`, `to`, `session` and `utc`: where the offer is gone or changed, (A), exit 2 with "the offer was dropped before the bind could be recorded, so the bind was NOT started: …" or its changed forms, the window renamed back first where it was renamed, no BINDING and nothing started; where it says a bind mode, `guard_bind_site`'s answer; where it says `allow`, 3 (section 5; 6253b0ed:22935–22943);
  - the hook's copy of the prefetch's `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms`, its first rewrite of the offer at answer 2, with the same requirement: the same three results, by the same `guard_answer_moved` (6253b0ed:20735–20744, :22908–22913). Where `.head` gives nothing to copy, it writes nothing and tests nothing, and the rewrite to `binding` makes the test;
  - the hook's back-out where no child started, `--binding`: nothing to back out, nothing written, and the refusal that says the offer was dropped or changed meanwhile; on an I/O failure, the record read again, and the refusal that says it still says binding until its deadline where it is still this answer's, or that this answer changed nothing more there where it is not (section 5; 00e2c92e:23496–23547);
  - `guard_bind_site`'s rewrites back to an offer, on the mode and attempt they loaded: nothing written, and `guard_site_moved`'s one answer, `guard_bind_gone`'s refusal where the record is gone (section 5; 6253b0ed:22419–22455);
  - choice 1's `mode=allow` write, tested by `guard_offer_still` on the answered offer just before its `mv`: where the offer is gone or changed, the mirror of (A), exit 2, nothing renamed or written; a bind mode, `guard_bind_site`'s answer; `allow`, 3 (section 5; lane 3's confirmations, #165 6886113842; 6253b0ed:22805–22818);
  - the outer's start: the rewrite's 2 since T502's part 21; nothing written, nothing run, and the outer's 2 (the table below);
  - the outer's outcome: the rewrite's 2, nothing written, and the outer's 0, its outcome in its `.out` alone (the table below).
- **`guard-bind`:**
  - The outer refuses unless the record says `mode=binding` (Y:5396), and sums `bound_s` from its `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` (CLI44:304; tasks:616).
  - Before `lane-start` `guard-bind` reads the offer file and, in phase 2 where the record holds the five copies, `start-record`'s pending records and F3's lookup before a rerun (Y:5511; tasks:1430–1431 at `b8e3df74`; lane 3's ruling, #165 6841918192, point 10). It makes the lookup only where one of those pending records still holds the copied `write_id`; where none does, there is no lookup and the bind runs anew (section 3; Y:5511; tasks:1430 at `f0c7d096`). Its read-door class stays `none` (Y:5399; tasks:673 at `b8e3df74`): the lookup is the in-doubt rule's call, not a read through the door. Y:5399's "only the offer file" is phase 1's.
  - At its end, where the body ended on its own, it reads the body's facts file and folds it into its outcome (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 6); where its bound or a signal stopped the body, it reads nothing the file holds. The outcome reaches the record only where its rewrite writes it, a 0, and otherwise stays in its `.out`. On every result it then removes the file, whether or not the outcome is written (section 1; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S1; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6882105713, S1). It finds its own `.out` by the record's `out`, and the facts file beside it (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233), from `out=` too, never from `bind` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118).
  - Just before it writes its outcome, and after it has gathered what the outcome writes, the five copies among them (lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S6), it reads the record again, and writes only where the record is still its own `binding`, with its own `pid` and `pidstart` (section 1; lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, point 18): the `pidstart` it wrote, or none where it wrote `pid` alone, on its own `host` and `container` (section 1; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 1; lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, points 19 and 20). Its rewrite makes the same test on the lines it copies (section 1; lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 21).
  - In phase 2, T510's child reads `act` for the `set-row-state` on the lane left, a call that always sends `LANES_LANE=<from>` and `LANES_EXPECT="row:<from> bind:<from>"`, and `LANES_ACT` only where the record holds an `act` (Y:5398; tasks:1421, :1438 at `f0c7d096`); the five copies for its lookup (tasks:1224); and `ended_utc`, `c` and `n_a` for its `w₀` wait (tasks:1225).
  - The body finds its facts file from the record's `out=`, never from `bind`. Where it cannot read the record itself, the outer hands it that path (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118).
  - **Its exits** (lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 6), as T502's code gives them. The outer reads the body's exit as the record's `rc` (section 3). This table is the source of two others (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 24):
    - `exit-codes.md`:271 leaves answer 2's codes to lane 5's section, and that section, which this PR adds after its :294, takes this table. Where the two differ, this table holds.
    - T502 has these exits join the file's exit table (tasks:673 at `f0c7d096`), and its hygiene table takes this one.
    - The code's two comments on the outer's exits leave out the 1 where the outcome cannot be written (code:570–572, :22450–22452, against :22680–22682). Lane 5's T502 round corrects them (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, its code follow-ups).

| Process | Exit | When, and the record after |
|---|---|---|
| `guard-bind <uuid>`, the outer | 2 | its usage, not one argument; an argument that is no safe name for `offers/<uuid>`; no record there, or one it cannot open; a record not in `mode=binding`; or one that already holds a `pid`, an outer before it. Nothing ran, and the record is as it was (code:22490–22493, :22497–22514, :31622) |
| | 2 | since T502's part 21, at its start: the record was gone at its start rewrite's test that it exists or at its open, or no longer said `mode=binding`. Nothing was written to it, and nothing ran: no record is made, and an offer that took the record's place is left as it is. Before part 21 that rewrite made the record again with the start's keys alone, or wrote them into the offer, and the bind ran (section 1; beeae571:20544, :20548–20550, :22694–22699; ae702c74:22666) |
| | 1 | the record could not be rewritten at its start. Nothing ran, and the guard reads the `binding`, which holds no `pid`, as section 5 gives (code:22543–22546; beeae571:22700–22703) |
| | 1 | the outcome could not be written: its rewrite's 1, an I/O failure (code:22680–22682; after T502's round 2, the rewrite's 1, section 1; lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S1), which since T502's part 16 is any failure of a write or of the record's open that the rewrite can see (ae702c74:20484–20545). This rewrite did not change the record; it is as another writer may have left it. The outcome is in its `.out` only, and its facts file is removed (section 1; lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S1; 12eddddf:22803–22806), where `bc9ed80b` leaves it |
| | 0 | the outcome is written: the bind's own code is the record's `rc`, a `TERM` its trap caught included, and its facts file is removed (code:22564, :22680–22686; 12eddddf:22808–22810) |
| | 0 | at its read before the outcome, the record was no longer its own `binding`. It is left as it was, the outcome is in its `.out` only, and its facts file is removed (section 1; code:22661–22665). After T502's round 2, the same where its rewrite's own test fails, the rewrite's 2, and where the record's `host` or `container` is not its own (section 1; lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, points 20 and 21; lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S1; a326c043:22766–22769; 12eddddf:22798–22801). Since T502's part 17, the same where the record is gone when the rewrite opens it, the rewrite's 2, which at `4fb88e68` was a 1 and this exit 1 (section 1; ae702c74:20522–20530, :22832–22835; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, finding 4) |
| | 128 + N | a signal that ends it, `KILL` among them: its trap catches `TERM` alone, and the hook starts it under `nohup`, which ignores `HUP` (code:22375, :22564). No outcome is written, and cases 2, 3 and 9 answer (section 5) |
| `guard-bind --body <uuid>`, the body | 2 | its usage, not two arguments; an argument that is no safe name for `offers/<uuid>`; or a record it cannot read, or not in `mode=binding`, with a `why=` saying so in its facts file where a valid facts path was supplied or derived, and none where it has no facts path (code:22878–22880, :22899–22902, :31617; lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S5, lane 5's choice of its two). Its other `why=` lines, below, are written on the same condition |
| | 3 or 2, in phase 2 | T510's lookup before `lane-start` (section 3): 3 where it settles nothing, a copy cannot make it, or the pending record found cannot be rerun or settled; 2 on LR007 `another-write` or `generation`; or the found rerun's own code. Each comes with its `why=` (code:22762–22862, :22913–22915) |
| | `lane-start`'s code | `lane-start --no-launch --bind` ended non-0, and its last line is the `why=` (code:22939–22944) |
| | 0 | `lane-start` ended 0. An act after it that fails, the lane left's `set-row-state` and the bound check among them, is a `note=`, never an exit (lane 3's ruling, #165 6841918192, point 4(b); code:22946–23046) |

- **Lane 4's T412** reads not the record but its liveness helper: the pending record's claims are live by `pid_start_of` (tasks:938; Y:5185).
- **Lane 2's AT-F6** (Ytp:5957–6259): twenty-two cases (R53; ERR-12), and four for rows the cases do not reach, which "write the offer file's record directly, with a sleeping process standing in for the child where one is needed" (Ytp:6202–6206). Its names are selected by T502's, T507's and T510's verifies (tasks:584, :708, :1215), and since spec#74's split by T514's. Its case 15 asserts the signal's code as 143 (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 11; Ytp:6130–6134).

## 8. Test seams

- **`LANES_BIND_BOUND_S`** overrides `bound_s` (section 4), and is honoured only as a whole number of at least 11 (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 10).
- **`LANES_BIND_BODY`** (R48; DEC-17; L5:535–538; tasks:583, :609, :613):
  - Where it is set, the outer runs it, with the uuid, in place of `"$RESOLVED" guard-bind --body`.
  - It is kept for the outer and unset in the body (tasks:622).
  - The body's `set-row-state` goes through `LANES_EDIT` where it is set, else `$RESOLVED`, as `lane-start`'s own writes do.
  - Lane 5 answers lane 2's point 39(b) with it (routed to lanes 5 and 4 by #165 6726110116), and lane 4's note (#165 6726296119) raises nothing on it (tasks:583).
- **The cases this contract asks of AT-F6 and T502's verify, or T514's since spec#74's split,** which the test plan's twenty-two cases and four rows do not yet make (Ytp:5957–6259; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 8). Lane 2 adds them to AT-F6, save those this section gives as T502's proof alone, and T502's selectors take them, or T514's where spec#74's split moved the function (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 24; lane 3's ruling (d'), #165 6903070719). Lane 2's T214x, `06bc34c3` (6893071430), adds to AT-F6 answer 2's and choice 1's cases where a `bound`, `bind-failed`, `allow` or other-mode record is in the offer's place, and answer 2's back-out where its binding is gone or no longer binding, as ten ids, each waiting for T502 and, since spec#74's split, for T514 (T214z's rows): in T214v's function, `[bound-at-its-rewrite]`, `[bind-failed-at-its-rewrite]`, `[allow-at-its-rewrite]` and `[another-mode-at-its-rewrite]`; in T214w's, `[bound-before-its-mv]`, `[bind-failed-before-its-mv]`, `[allow-before-its-mv]` and `[another-mode-before-its-mv]`; and `test_answer_2s_back_out_writes_nothing_where_its_binding_is_gone_or_no_longer_binding[gone-at-its-back-out|not-binding-at-its-back-out]`. Lane 2's runs on `6dd8bbbd` pass the three functions' 16 ids, 16 of 16, and the whole guard and gate selection passes 236 of 236 on line R's future tree. It builds no back-out id for choice 1, whose branch forks no child (6893071430). Point 26's cases and ruling 5's others are T502's proof alone (lane 2's check of draft 19, #165 6892644761). Where a case needs the outer to do what the shipped script will not do on its own, it runs from a test-only copy of `lanes-edit.sh`, and no seam goes into the shipped script (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 23); and point 26's race stays T502's proof alone, with no AT-F6 slice, unless lane 3 rules otherwise (lane 5's answer to lane 2's question in #165 6892644761):
  - **An outer that writes `pid` alone,** its own `pid_start_of $$` made to exit 2 by the test-only copy: with a stub `lane-start` that exits 0, the record holds `pid` and no `pidstart`, ends `bound`, and the next prompt says "MOVED"; with one that exits non-0, it ends `bind-failed` and case 6 answers (section 1).
  - **A record that is no longer the outer's own** when it reads it again: rewritten, while the stub `lane-start` sleeps, to another `pid`, to a `binding` with no `pid`, or to a fresh `name-drift` offer. The outer leaves it as it is, exits 0, and its `.out` holds the outcome (section 1). So it does where the record names another `host` or `container` with the outer's own `pid` and no `pidstart` (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 20).
  - **The narrowed window** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 21): a test-only copy pauses the outer after its read, and the test then installs a new record. The rewrite's own test leaves that record as it is, and the outer's outcome goes to its `.out` alone. The test also asserts that `guard_bind_copies` ran before the outer's last ownership read (lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S6).
  - **The case ids** these cases take (lane 3's Codex re-review, #165 6880414089, S9). Lane 5 names them, lane 2 confirms T214's, and lane 3 folds them into AT-F6, T502's selector (since spec#74's split, S2 alone; S1, S3, S4 and S6 are T514's) and, where a case replays in phase 2, T510's:
    - S1, the rewrite's three results: `test_the_outcome_rewrite_has_three_results[written]`, `[io-failure]` and `[not-its-own]`, each asserting the outer's exit, where the outcome is, and that no facts file is left (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6880978929, S1): `[written]`, the outer's 0 and the outcome in the record; `[io-failure]`, the outer's 1, the record not changed by the rewrite, and the outcome in the `.out` alone; `[not-its-own]`, the outer's 0, the record left as it is, and the outcome in the `.out` alone.
    - S2, `host` and `container`: `test_the_outer_matches_host_and_container_without_regard_to_case[case-only-host]` and `[case-only-container]`, which write the outcome, and `[other-host]` and `[other-container]`, real mismatches with the outer's own `pid` and no `pidstart`, which leave the record.
    - S3, the canonical `out`: `test_a_noncanonical_out_names_no_facts_file_and_no_words[bind-0]` and `[bind-01]`, for `.bind.0.out` and `.bind.01.out`.
    - S4, the files' index: `test_the_files_index_passes_six_digits[999999]` and `[1000000]`, each asserting the next attempt's `out` one above it.
    - S6, the order: `test_the_outer_gathers_its_copies_before_its_last_ownership_read`.
  - **Lane 2's names, which lane 5 takes** (lane 2's answers, #165 6885568373; lane 2's case, #165 6885773752; lane 5's post, #165 6885608994; T502's parts 16, 17, 19, 20 and 21; lane 3's Codex code review, #165 6882203066, findings 1, 4 and 10).
