Each answers "BINDING IN PROGRESS" with no pid until `deadline_epoch`, then case 9 with no session test, and no `kill`, `pgrep -s` or `pid_start_of` call is made with the value (section 5).
  - **The rewrites:** each leaves the record 0600, even one that was 0644 before it, and a new `offers/` 0700. Where a write fails, this writer did not change the record, and the case tests only that: with no other writer in the case, the record's bytes after the failure are those it had before, which is not a promise that the record is as it was where another writer runs (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6885771975, S3; section 1). Where the case injects a write failure and leaves the removal able to succeed, no temporary file is left in `offers/`; the removal is best effort, and no case asserts it where the removal itself fails (lane 3's Codex delta review, #165 6885771975, S4; section 1).
  - **The grammar:** a key given twice is read by its first line; a key no reader knows survives every rewrite; `bounded=` with any value but `no` reads as `yes`; and a `bound` record whose `rc` is not 0 is case 11 (section 3).
  - **The 540 s and 510 s bands:** with one address and `c` = 3, `w_s_ms` = 473 gives `bound_s=530` and 474 gives 540; and with one address and `c` = 2, T214p's `[c2-w368]` and `[c2-w369]`, on line R, 368 gives `bound_s=500` and 369 gives 510 (section 4; lane 2's check of draft 18, #165 6891950000).
  - **The exits:** each row of section 7's table, for the outer and the body.
  - **No lookup without a pending record:** in phase 2, a record whose copied `write_id` no pending record still holds makes no `journal` call in the shim's log and no `w₀` wait, and binds anew (section 3).

## 9. Notes: Y beside W, and the rulings followed over Y's text

- **Where Y differs from W on a passage used here:** two lines of the bound's sum, W:5407–5408 and Y:5458–5459. Y adds the closed form "437 + 28`N_a`·`c` + 19`w_s` s, rounded up to 10 s", the p90 reading ("`c` = 3 means a p90 round trip under about 276 ms"), the 540 s band, and the 590 s at Eagle's p90 (lane 5's O3, #165 6725361742). This contract follows Y. Every other line of W:5336–5512 is Y:5387–5563 unchanged, and W:5149 is Y:5200.
- **R05 (ERR-05):** `guard-bind` reads no configuration. It sums `bound_s` from the `c`, `n_a` and `w_s_ms` that the hook copies from `.head` into the record (CLI44:304–305). Y:5877's "from the configuration", and Ytp:6193's, are read so.
- **O15** (CLI44:111, :703–704; B2:138–143): the key is `w_s_ms`, in whole milliseconds, since `guard-bind` is bash 3.2. `c` is the larger of the two URLs' `connect_timeout=`, which "only lengthens T510's wait".
- **T510's `C`** for `w₀` is `N_a`·`c` + 6 s, from the record's `c` and `n_a` (tasks:1225; L5:703). Y:5511 states it as 8 s, its value at `c` = 2 with one address.
- **R47 (ERR-10):** the outer sweeps `timeout`'s group (section 4), where Y:5401 has `timeout` send the `KILL`.
- **R48 (DEC-17):** `LANES_BIND_BODY` (section 8).
- **R49:** `pid_start_of`'s exit 2 is live (section 6), recorded here.
- **R52 (ERR-11):** the child also unsets `LANES_WRITE_GENERATION`, the printed rerun's variable (Y:5200), with `LANES_WRITE_ID` (tasks:623). The child's environment is otherwise Y:5410–5415's (tasks:619–624).
- **R53 (ERR-12):** AT-F6 has twenty-two cases.
- **R58 (ERR-14):** "BINDING" for answer 2, and "BINDING IN PROGRESS" while the bind runs.
- **R50:** plan.md lists this file (plan.md:274), and the skeleton's lane 5 block gains it. It lands after the spec PR and before T502 (tasks:569, :571).
- **Lane 3's rulings on draft 1's open points** (#165 6746215510). Y's frozen text keeps its words, and lane 3's errata go into `research.md` Part 1 in B4:
  - **ERR-23** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 7; numbered in B4, #165 6747379291, research:359–367): Y:5503's and Y:5505's "Record after" add "window renamed back to `<from>`", and Y:5506's renames nothing. This contract follows the erratum (sections 2 and 5). Since T502's part 62, case 10 renames back where this answer renamed the window, with the exceptions in section 5, as case 9 does: the rename it undoes is this answer's own, on this host, not the other host's bind's, by lane 3's ruling (#165 6898801339; section 5, case 10's row).
  - **ERR-24, two citation errata** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6746215510, point 14; numbered in B4, #165 6747379291, research:369–375): Y:5507's `:11097–11101` reads `:11091–11094`, and Y:5428's `:10965–10973` reads `:10963–10971`. Both hold at the pin (sections 3 and 5).
  - **Four rulings change what Y's text gives,** and lane 3's analysis names no erratum for them:
    - point 4: Y:5394's fork sends its own output to `/dev/null`, which leaves the hook no `$!`. The ruling returns `$!` from inside the fork through a command substitution, with `$detach` as a bash array (section 1);
    - point 6: the facts file is a second file per attempt, beside Y:5408's "one 0600 file per attempt" (section 1);
    - point 8: `detach` is a key that Y:5425–5432 does not list (section 3);
    - point 9: "Y:5499's text no longer holds once the outer is dead", in lane 3's words, so case 3 takes its own text there (section 5).
  - **The other rulings,** points 1, 2, 3, 5, 10, 11, 12 and 13, fill gaps Y leaves, and change none of its words.
- **Lane 3's rulings on draft 2's points** (#165 6747528875, with #165 6747722621 narrowing point 15). Lane 3 names no erratum for them:
  - point 15: Y gives no rule for a malformed `pid` or `pidstart`. The ruling takes Y:5505's own fallback, the wait for `deadline_epoch` (section 5);
  - point 16: (b) says how Y:5488's "stopped by its bound" is told from the body's own end, refining point 11; (c) gives a `why` where Y:5487's refusal line is missing; (a) and (d) fill gaps (sections 1 and 3);
  - points 6 and 13: the facts file's two removers are one rule, and case 11 leaves `<uuid>.bind.*` (section 1). Y:5507's "dropped" is unchanged.
- **Lane 3's rulings on draft 3's points** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589, with lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233, replacing its answer to point 18's question 1). Round B8 or B9 records them in `research.md` "as decisions, or as errata where a Y line needs one", and B8 takes the numbers in order (lane 3's ruling, #165 6751772589):
  - **ERR-43, the erratum to Y:5394** (lane 3's correction, #165 6751818233; numbered in round B8, research:640–653 at B8; research:773–790 at `b8e3df74`): Y:5394's `<k>` becomes the files' index, one above the highest index among the uuid's `.bind.*` files and 1 where there are none, and `bind` keeps the attempt, 1 to 3 per offer (Y:5429). `out` is already a key of the record (Y:5431), so the erratum names no new key. B8's ERR-43 recorded the files' index, and said the facts file sits beside the `out` file. Round B9 (`80f824e6`) added lane 3's rule on the facts file to it, so at `b8e3df74` ERR-43 records that rule too (research:782–785 and :788–789 at `b8e3df74`), and tasks:655 at `b8e3df74` says so: the facts file is `out=`'s `<offers>/<uuid>.bind.<k>` stem with `.facts`, which the outer, the body and the guard's sweep all find from `out=`, never from `bind`, the outer handing the body that path where the body cannot read the record itself (lane 3's ruling, #165 6752064118). That rule names no new key either. This contract follows the erratum (sections 1, 3 and 7), and the rule with it (sections 1 and 7);
  - point 17: Y sets no length for a `pid`. The ruling takes `pid_start_of`'s cap of 10 digits into the record's rule (sections 5 and 6);
  - point 18, question 2: Y:5396 and Y:5486–5488 have the outer write its outcome. The ruling has it read the record again first, and write only over its own `binding` (section 1);
  - the two readings: lane 3 confirms both, the record with `pid` alone (section 5) and point 16(b) sorting only the `why` (section 4). Neither changes a Y line.
- **Lane 3's ruling on T510's point 10** (lane 3's ruling, #165 6841918192, point 10). Lane 3 names no erratum for it, and `research.md` at `b8e3df74` holds none: Y:5399's "only the offer file" is phase 1's, and before `lane-start` phase 2 also reads `start-record`'s pending records, and F3's lookup before a rerun (Y:5511), with the read-door class still `none` (section 7). Who drops the five copies follows Y:5431 and Y:5516–5519 (section 3).
- **Y:5500's files-mode half** is read as `design.md:5547` at `f0c7d096` corrects it (edited in place by `88c10cc`; lane 4, #165 6833596198; research:136 at `f0c7d096`): in files mode two binds that both read the lane free both land (section 5, case 4; lane 3's review, #165 6868034845, B-1). The primary half stands.
- **svc:142** has the offer file record the bind "with the child's pid and start time". Under lane 3's ruling, #165 6747528875, point 15, the outer writes no start where `pid_start_of $$` exits 2, and it still writes its outcome, as svc:148–154 need (section 1; lane 3's Codex review, #165 6877006381, finding 1). This contract reads svc:142's start time as "where it can be read". Lane 3 amends the scenario: "spec#48's svc:142 reads "the child's pid, and its start time where it can be read". It goes into the follow-up's commit 20." (lane 3's ruling, #165 6879754335, point 22).

With that confirmation, ruling 5 ("No rewrite creates a record") and point 18's question 1 hold outside each rewrite's window, as section 1 and ruling 5's row say:

| Point | Ruled | Lands in |
|---|---|---|
| 27 | accepted as limits for v1.3, in lane 3's words "each stated in the contract with its consequence, in point 26's form", which covers the facts file under a reused index (part 52) and the FIFO window's processes (part 54), the FIFO window's consequence stated as it is, in part 54's comment and in section 1, lane 2's NIT-7 first option (lane 3's rulings, #165 6894116246): from `ln` to a child link's removal; the `noclobber` fallback's empty instant, its status 4's test to its `rm`, and its test that nothing is there to its open, where a FIFO also leaves the temporary file in `offers/` and the prompt goes however Claude Code treats a hook that its timeout ends, and where that timeout signals only the hook's top process, the subshell and the blocked writer survive until they are killed or the FIFO is opened for reading, which signal Claude Code sends being not verified; each rewrite's read to its `mv`; choice 1's check to its `mv`, which is not closed; case 5's, `guard_drop_if`'s and `guard_allow_drop`'s reads to their `rm`, with case 5's and choice 1's consequences under a reused index, a newer attempt's `binding` removed, its live `.out` too where `.bind.1.out` was listed, and choice 1's ALLOWED told for an offer that is no longer there; the facts file's name under a reused index, where an older outer folds a newer attempt's account into its own unlinked outcome and removes the file, and the newer outcome says the body gave no account or no reason; and each "still" after an I/O failure, true at its re-read only. "None of the three proposals is built for v1.3", in lane 3's words. The first is a per-record claim: `mkdir "<record>.lock"` holding its pid and start time, taken by every rewriter across its check and its `mv`, with a dead owner's claim taken over, as `tests/run.sh`'s fallback does. It would close choice 1's window and the rewrites', at the cost of a new stuck state on the prompt path; lane 5's T502 writer judged it not needed if lane 3 accepts the windows, since nothing was then thought to be unlinked (lane 5's T502 writer's report), which lane 5's adversarial review of T502's parts 42 to 47 shows false under a reused index. The two narrower proposals, not built for v1.3, are that review's: (b) each listed name hard-linked to a private name before the read, and a name removed only where it is still that file (`-ef`), which narrows the risk to one test; and (c) files' indices that never repeat after a sweep, as with a floor of epoch seconds. Lane 1's R1 adds these, which that ruling does not name, and which lane 3 confirms under it (#165 6894714082): in each rewrite's window, a record removed made again by the `mv`, at `binding` by the hook's rewrite and at `allow` by choice 1, which says ALLOWED, and two answers `2` to one offer starting two outers on one `.out`, the second fork's `>` emptying the first's, the record naming only the outer whose start rewrite lands last, the other's outcome going only to that `.out`, one body's `why=` and `note=` lost or folded into the other's outcome through their one facts file, and what two `lane-start --no-launch --bind` runs for one transcript do at once not known, reasoned and not run (SHOULD-1; lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, SHOULD 2); case 11's drops, an unconditional `rm -f` on a read apart from the one acted on, which can drop an offer restored meanwhile, so that its attempts count afresh, or a live `binding`, leaving its `.out` (SHOULD-2), which lane 3 has since ruled a fix (#165 6894385553) and T502's part 55 builds, leaving the window from the load, or `guard_answer`'s reads, to the drop's `rm`, stated with its cost by part 58; where the `noclobber` fallback's empty instant is dropped after the writer's test, the offer said made while the record is gone, and where its status 4 removes a `binding`, that outer running nothing or its outcome going only to its `.out`; and choice 1's S3 note saying the allowance is recorded where the outer's start rewrite writes `binding` over it (NIT-1); and the dangling link's `rm`, from its test to its `rm`, which can remove another prompt's offer, or a `binding` made from it, whose outer then runs nothing or writes its outcome only to its `.out`, the retry then making this prompt's offer in its place, or, where a record is there again, refusing the prompt (R1's answer 3; lane 3's Opus delta review of draft 21, NIT 5). Lane 3 rules R1's NIT-3 apart, as a platform limit for v1.3, stated in point 26's form, with nothing built for v1.3: where hard links are refused in `offers/` and `/dev/fd/3` cannot be `stat()`ed, a first mismatch prompt of `1`, `2` or `3` is acted on as an answer to a question never shown (lane 3's rulings, #165 6894714082; section 1). Its proposals, not built for v1.3: a claim by rename, the record read renamed to a private name first, which works only where it is still there, what was claimed checked, then the new record renamed into place, which is the `mkdir` claim in another form; and for case 11's drops, a drop made only on a fresh read that still fails, or by that claim, in whose place part 55's fix is built. T502's parts 57 to 59, at `de96ef86`, state in their comments two windows this point already holds, the index's reuse (proposal (c); lane 3's review of `12eddddf..00e2c92e`, NIT 5), with an index that never repeats as the way to close it, not built, and `ln`'s, with why `link` is not taken; and one new one: the window an answer `2` leaves named for `<to>` where the bind record it met did not end in a bind, until it is renamed by hand (sections 1 and 5), save case 10 in a window this answer renamed, past `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s and after its rewrite back, with the exceptions in section 5, which lane 3's ruling on T502's case 10 takes out of it: there case 10 renames back, as case 9 does (#165 6898801339); T502's part 62, `6d2bc47a`, builds that, and its rename back leaves case 9's window (section 5, case 10's row). Case 10's other paths stay inside this window: before `deadline_epoch` plus 60 s it answers as in progress and keeps a window this answer renamed, and on its rewrite back's 2 `guard_site_moved` keeps it for a record that is gone or unreadable, an offer or an allowance from another lane, any other mode or a second bind record, as part 59's comment states; the next prompt starts with `GBT_REN_FROM` empty, so where that other host's bind then leaves no outcome, case 10 past its deadline renames nothing, and the window keeps `<to>`'s name until it is renamed by hand (lane 3's delta review of follow-up 2's part 1, SHOULD-1, spec#70 6901055715; lane 3's delta review of follow-up 3's part 1, NIT-1, #165 6915955296; 1604ef38:23149–23156, :22784–22796, :22813–22815, :22832–22834, :22843–22848, :22368, :23180–23181, :23201). T502's part 60, at `f2e545b8`, states one more, also new: within one prompt, where the stale drop failed and the offer site's retry then meets a record another prompt put there meanwhile, the retry's refusal names the failed `rm` though the record there is that newer one, and the next prompt reads it (section 1). T502's part 61, at `475a04a4`, states one more: (A)'s rename back, kept where the record read just after this answer's rewrite is no bind record, undoes a bind's rename where, inside a stalled hook, that bind ran, ended `bound` and was heard and dropped, and the next prompt meets a window named for `<from>` in a lane now `<to>` (section 5). Lane 3 accepts these three new windows, part 59's, part 60's `GA_DROP_FAILED` and part 61's, as limits for v1.3, since each needs a second hook, or a stalled one, on the same offer (spec#65 6898800291, lane 3's call (b); #165 6898801339). T502's part 62's back-out keep, for another host's or container's bind record, is a limit for v1.3 too, as lane 3 rules: the keep reads the record's mode only, and the veto 2 it avoids is a bind's on this host only, so there the window is kept named for `<to>` where renaming back would meet no veto 2; a later part may test `host=` and `container=` (lane 3's delta review of part 62, NIT-3, #165 6899381832; section 5, the back-out) | sections 1, 5 and 10 |

Every point is ruled at draft 21: T502's part 22, built as parts 22 to 26 and completed by parts 27 to 54 at `00e2c92e`, builds point 26's new ruling and ruling 5, and `guard_allow_drop`'s exception to point 13's sweep needs no code change (section 1).
